#2209. What drone footage revealed about Mauricio Pochettino's Man United tactics: How he'll set up with and without the ball, a change of role for Bryan Mbeumo and his intriguing use of Joshua Zirkzee

Published: 07:00 EST, 20 November 2026 | Updated: 07:10 EST, 20 November 2026

While thinking over the last few days about his first press conference as Manchester United boss, Mauricio Pochettino would not have expected drones and the new Grand Theft Auto video game to be top of the agenda.

Having touched down in Manchester on Wednesday, taking a private jet laid on by United in the immediate aftermath of the United States’ game in Cincinnati against Haiti, it has been a whirlwind 48 hours for Pochettino and his staff.

On Thursday, he took charge of training for the first time against the backdrop of question marks around Bryan Mbeumo’s decision to post late into the night about popular new video game GTA VI.

By the time the day ended, the issue at hand was far more pressing as a drone pilot filmed and uploaded to YouTube a 12-minute video from Pochettino’s first session at Carrington.

How worried Pochettino actually is about the footage – which has since been pulled from YouTube, with a grovelling apology issued – remains to be seen but only this summer did the Argentine concede that ‘we are in an era of spies’.

The footage wasn’t a tactical golden ticket for Liverpool, but what was there to take from it?

A drone pilot filmed and uploaded to YouTube a 12-minute video from Mauricio Pochettino’s first training session as Manchester United manager

The players get to work, with United's coaching staff seemingly unaware that their every move is being followed from the skies

Pochettino sides have had a consistent set-up with and without the ball over the years.

The Argentine and his staff have, more often than not, leaned into a back-three in possession, shifting to a back-four without the ball.

In the drone footage we could see the United squad divided into three groups with one XI out on a different training pitch with Pochettino and coach Toni Jimenez.

That group were lined up in what appeared to be that preferred in-possession set-up, with academy defender Reece Munro tucking in from right back to form a back-three alongside Harry Maguire and Ayden Heaven, traditional centre backs in a back-four.

Pochettino likes to have at least one full back push on extremely high to provide the width and in this snippet of his first training session, that responsibility landed on the shoulders of teenager Harry Amass, who has struggled for minutes this season, getting a total of just 29 under Michael Carrick.

The United players are put into position by Pochettino at Carrington

Similarly, Pochettino has often shown a desire to play No 10s – or attacking midfielders – quite narrow. And that is what we saw here, with Amass and Mbeumo providing the width. Mbeumo was on the right of a 4-2-3-1 shape with Matheus Cunha and 17-year-old academy talent Jaume Camacho playing narrower and Joshua Zirkzee up front.

The formation becomes a 3-2-4-1 in possession with the full back pushing on to the wing, creating two No 10s behind the lone striker.

The footage was significant in that it showed Pochettino is implementing his ideas from day one and is wasting no time in trying to embed a style he needs players to get up to speed with quickly.

But little should be read into the personnel given it was an amalgamation of young players and senior regulars.

We could see academy players Amass, Camacho and Munro among the 10 outfield players, along with Cunha, Heaven, Maguire, Kobbie Mainoo, Mbeumo, Andrey Santos and Zirkzee.

Of the 10 that were put to task by Pochettino, only four have started more than five league games this season.

It is highly improbable that the personnel used will be anywhere close to the starting XI at Anfield on Sunday.

The footage also showed games of one-touch rondos going on between the two other groups which were divided between the rest of Pochettino’s coaching team, which includes his son Sebastiano and Miguel D’Agostino, as well as trusted assistant Jesus Perez.

Pochettino greets his new players including Bryan Mbeumo, who lined up out wide in the first training session

United's players start a 'rondo', where one defender stands in the middle and tries to pinch the ball from the players stood around the edge - who are allowed one touch at a time

One group included Diogo Dalot, Leny Yoro, Lisandro Martinez, Matthijs de Ligt, Youri Tielemans, Mason Mount and Noussair Mazraoui. The other was academy-led with Bruno Fernandes leading a rondo that featured Shea Lacey, Jack and Tyler Fletcher, Dan Armer, as well as Jaydan Kamason.

But the use of Mbeumo in Pochettino’s work was one of the most interesting aspects, largely because he was back operating in a wider role.

With injuries hampering Benjamin Sesko, Mbeumo has had to spend a lot of the season deployed through the middle. That could well have to continue given Sesko is not anticipated to return until late January, but Pochettino’s decision to use Zirkzee as the figurehead in his set-up was intriguing.

Pochettino’s principles and set-up won’t vary too much; the onus now is on players to adapt. But Pochettino and Jimenez did look to have players in the correct positions. There were no obvious experiments – which makes sense when time is of the essence.

Amass plays off the left, Munro is a centre back, Maguire is comfortable in the middle of any back three, Camacho plays as a No 10 in the academy, both Mainoo and Santos have played together in a double pivot at the base of midfield and Zirkzee is a centre forward.

Pochettino likes a centre forward who can drop in to allow No 10s to run beyond and that suits Zirkzee’s style well, allowing him to be involved as a link man and not isolated with his back to goal.

Mbeumo works better running in behind as a counter-attacking striker, playing on the shoulder of the last defender.

How Pochettino uses Mbeumo over the longer term remains curious because swap out Camacho for Fernandes and Amass for Dorgu and there is a plausible set-up for success, with Mbeumo holding width on the right, Cunha and Fernandes in those central pockets they crave most, and Dorgu holding width off the left.

Lisandro Martinez (left) and Leny Yoro also meet Pochettino and his staff at Carrington

Pochettino’s track record of working with and improving young players became immediately apparent, with so many given an introduction to his staff and their methods on day one.

While both Jack and Tyler Fletcher, along with Amass, Lacey and Bendito Mantato have made their first-team debuts, it was a golden opportunity in particular for Camacho, the stand-out player in the academy this season following the exit of JJ Gabriel.

United routinely promote multiple academy players each day to bolster numbers in first-team training sessions and it’s a good way for staff to bridge the gap in speed, physicality and intensity between senior and youth football.

Every manager that comes in likes to take on a project and when Ruben Amorim walked through the door it was Godwill Kukonki that caught the eye of staff.

Gabriel became a training mainstay in the months prior to his bombshell request to terminate his contract, and now Camacho, Munro and Armer are working towards first-team debuts instead.

#2207. Lazio-Roma, biglietti del derby: vendita libera dalle 11 del 24 novembre

Si apre la terza fase per la gara del 13 dicembre; nella vendita libera una persona può acquistare fino a quattro tagliandi.

La vendita libera dei biglietti per Lazio-Roma inizierà martedì 24 novembre alle 11:00. Secondo quanto riportato da Sslazio, Si tratta della terza fase di vendita per il derby, in programma domenica 13 dicembre alle 12:30, come comunicato dalla S.S. Lazio.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

Dalle 11:00 del 24 novembre la vendita sarà aperta per tutto lo stadio. Per i posti dell’intera Tribuna Tevere resterà però obbligatorio possedere una fidelity card Eagle Card o Millenovecento. Per procedere online occorrerà inserire il codice della card come coupon; il codice potrà anche essere presentato al momento dell’acquisto.

Ai biglietti acquistati online sarà applicata una commissione di servizio del 3,80%. I possessori di una fidelity card Millenovecento o Eagle che non sono abbonati potranno chiedere, anche acquistando online, che il titolo d’ingresso sia caricato elettronicamente sulla card.

I biglietti ridotti per invalidi civili al 100% e quelli di Tribuna Tevere per persone non deambulanti, in entrambi i casi con accompagnatore, potranno essere acquistati soltanto nei Lazio Style 1900, escluso il punto vendita di Parco Leonardo. Il ridotto Under 16 è riservato ai ragazzi nati dal 1° gennaio 2010.

Chi compra fisicamente un biglietto in un punto vendita deve mostrare un documento d’identità: senza, il titolo di accesso non può essere emesso, secondo il D.L. 8/2/2010 n. 8. Non sono considerate idonee alla vendita le patenti di ultima generazione che non riportano il luogo di residenza.

Nella vendita libera ogni persona può acquistare al massimo quattro biglietti, secondo le disposizioni dell’Osservatorio sulle Manifestazioni Sportive. Per gli altri tre eventuali intestatari è sufficiente presentare anche una copia del documento d’identità. I bambini di quattro anni nati dal 1° gennaio 2022 entrano gratuitamente e senza biglietto, mostrando un documento d’identità o la tessera sanitaria. All’ingresso l’identità sarà controllata per ogni spettatore: il documento è obbligatorio anche per i minorenni.

#2199. Liverpool fan found not guilty of racially abusing Antoine Semenyo as Man City star withdraws support for prosecution

Published: 05:49 EST, 20 November 2026 | Updated: 08:12 EST, 20 November 2026

A Liverpool fan accused of racially abusing footballer Antoine Semenyo has been found not guilty after the player withdrew his support for the prosecution.

Semenyo was due to give evidence at Liverpool Magistrates' Court on Friday in the trial of Mark Mogan, 47, who was accused of shouting abuse during the opening match of the Premier League season at Anfield on August 15 last year.

But Simon Jones, prosecuting, said: 'Following recent contact made with Mr Semenyo, he has indicated he is no longer supportive of proceedings and the prosecution do not consider it is appropriate to compel him.

'In those circumstances we offer no further evidence in the case.' District Judge Mary Dowrick recorded a verdict of not guilty to the charge of racially aggravated behaviour.

Semenyo, who was a Bournemouth player at the time of the incident but later signed for Manchester City, had been due to give evidence last month but the trial was adjourned halfway through when Mr Mogan was taken to hospital after suffering a seizure.

The court had heard Mr Mogan, in a wheelchair, shouted at Semenyo as he went to take a throw-in about 30 minutes into the game.

Mark Mogan (right) has been found not guilty of racially abusing Antoine Semenyo (left) after the player withdrew his support for the prosecution

He was alleged to have said: 'Hurry up and take the throw-in, you black b*****d.'

Opening the case last month, Mr Jones said: 'That racial abuse was shouted directly at Antoine Semenyo, who was close to him.

'Mr Semenyo briefly froze, as he couldn't believe what the male had said.' Mr Mogan then shouted "I'm talking to you, you ****", the court heard.

Witnesses also said they saw him throw his chewing gum at the footballer.

Semenyo reported the incident to the match referee and Mr Mogan was removed from the stadium at half-time.

Mr Mogan, who had an intermediary with him in court, told police in interview that Semenyo had called him a cripple after he shouted at the player to 'hurry up'.

Mr Mogan, of Templehill Close in Dovecot, Liverpool, declined to comment as he left court.

Semenyo, who scored a brace in the game, had brought the incident to the attention of match referee Anthony Taylor and the match was briefly halted while both managers and captains were spoken to.

At half time an announcement was made to the crowd asking for no racist abuse and pictures on social media showed a man in a wheelchair being escorted along the touchline and out of the ground.

Mogan, pictured with Liverpool legend Robbie Fowler, would have been banned for life if he'd have been found guilty

Merseyside Police later announced the man had been arrested and then was bailed for three months pending further investigations.

Pictures on social media showed Mogan playing wheelchair basketball for a team called the Greenbank Meteors while other images show him posing with Liverpool legends Robbie Fowler and Emile Heskey.

After he was charged, Liverpool confirmed he would be banned for life if found guilty but Judge Dowrick recorded a verdict of not guilty on Friday morning.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

#2197. Accolto il ricorso, il Tas riassegna la Coppa d'Africa al Senegal

(ANSA) - ROMA, 24 NOV - Il Senegal torna campione d'Africa. Il Tribunale Arbitrale dello Sport ha infatti riassegnato all'unanimità la contestata Coppa del 2025, ribaltando la decisione che aveva attribuito al Marocco il trofeo a tavolino. Il 17 marzo, la commissione d'appello della Confederazione Africana di Calcio aveva revocato il titolo al Senegal, assegnandolo al Marocco, dopo una finale segnata da incidenti ma comunque terminata. Il Senegal a sua volta aveva presentato ricorso al Tas che, a più di dieci mesi dalla finale, ha ritenendo che l'arbitro non avesse commesso alcun errore nell'ordinare la ripresa del gioco dopo gli incidenti. Il 18 gennaio a Rabat, il Senegal aveva vinto la Coppa d'Africa battendo 1-0 il Marocco dopo i tempi supplementari e il caos, per la contestazione di un rigore assegnato al Marocco nei minuti di recupero.

La sentenza di primo grado aveva solo comminato sanzioni disciplinari, poi in appello il ko a tavolino per il Senegal, reo di aver "abbandonato il campo prima della fine prevista della partita senza il permesso dell'arbitro". La Federcalcio senegalese aveva denunciato "il più sfacciato furto nella storia del nostro sport". Ora il successo al Tas e la Coppa che torna al Senegal. Il Marocco può ancora ricorrere alla Corte federale svizzera, che non si pronuncia sul merito ma interviene sulle questioni procedurali. (ANSA).

#2192. Sinner shock, operazione al ginocchio: rientra in primavera

Il tennista sarà operato a Turku il 24 novembre; le prime stime indicano un recupero di circa tre-quattro mesi.

Jannik Sinner ha deciso di sottoporsi a un intervento al ginocchio destro. L’operazione è programmata per martedì 24 novembre a Turku, in Finlandia, e sarà eseguita dal professor Lasse Lempainen. La scelta arriva dopo diversi consulti medici e oltre quattro mesi di terapie conservative che non hanno dato i risultati sperati.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

Le prime stime indicano un recupero di circa tre-quattro mesi. Il rientro potrebbe avvenire in primavera, ma i tempi dipenderanno dall’esito dell’intervento e dalla successiva riabilitazione.

Il 12 luglio Sinner aveva conquistato il suo secondo Wimbledon consecutivo battendo Alexander Zverev in finale. Quella partita è stata anche la sua ultima apparizione ufficiale della stagione. Nelle settimane successive il tennista italiano ha cominciato ad avvertire dolore al ginocchio destro.

Il problema è stato identificato come una sindrome della bandelletta ileotibiale, che provoca dolore nella parte esterna dell’articolazione. Per un atleta sottoposto a sollecitazioni continue può diventare particolarmente fastidiosa.

All’inizio Sinner e il suo gruppo di lavoro avevano scelto di evitare la chirurgia. Il percorso prevedeva riposo, fisioterapia e trattamenti specifici, con l’obiettivo di recuperare gradualmente la piena funzionalità del ginocchio senza correre rischi inutili o compromettere ulteriormente la carriera del tennista.

Nonostante i tentativi e i consulti con diversi specialisti, il dolore non è scomparso definitivamente. Il numero due del mondo ha quindi preso in considerazione l’intervento. Decisivo è stato l’ultimo confronto con Lempainen, chirurgo ortopedico finlandese specializzato nel trattamento delle lesioni muscolari e tendinee degli sportivi professionisti.

Tra gli atleti già seguiti dal medico figurano anche i calciatori Ousmane Dembélé e Leonardo Spinazzola. Dopo la valutazione della situazione clinica di Sinner, la scelta è ricaduta sull’operazione a Turku.

© 2015 LazioPress.it | Tutti i diritti sono riservati | Testata giornalistica con autorizzazione del Tribunale Civile di Roma numero 13/2015.

#2188. St Mirren boss McLeish claims 'self-interest' is hampering Scottish football development

Published: 09:55 EST, 20 November 2026 | Updated: 09:55 EST, 20 November 2026

St Mirren manager Craig McLeish claims self-interest and poor governance are killing Scottish football’s hopes of improvement.

McLeish is frustrated that Scottish Football Association chief football officer Craig Mulholland has reportedly had to dilute plans for young player quotas to appease five William Hill Premiership clubs.

Mulholland was hoping to ensure teams featured Scotland Under-21 eligible players for an average of 60 minutes per league game and had two on the pitch for each cup tie.

McLeish said: 'That’s damning that he’s having to change his plans to pander to clubs who aren’t really on board with it and don’t really have the best interests of the Scottish national team at heart.

'I understand why. We have owners from outwith this country. We have managers from outwith this country. They’re in jobs, they’re all fighting for their clubs.

'But why then are those people allowed to be the ones that hold the keys to the future of our country and the development of our national team?

St Mirren boss Craig McLeish believes self-interest at clubs is hampering Scottish football

'It’s the way governance is set up and we’ve killed ourselves with that because the people who actually have a say in it have, not have no interest but they’ve got self-interest, and there’s a lot of self-interest at the top end.

'I just don’t see a way that we can really properly change unless something to do with governance changes and I just don’t ever see that happening. It’s really sad.'

Although McLeish backs quotas, he fears that would not address the root cause of Scottish football’s problems.

'We always focus at the top end, what our league size is, quotas for young players, a national base,' said the 36-year-old, who worked in St Mirren’s youth academy before his current role.

'We have a fundamental problem that starts at our youngest age groups, at four and five years of age, about how we actually teach kids to play. And until you fix that, you can’t fix the top end of it.

'It’s crazy some of the stuff that’s going on, the formats, how we teach kids. There’s just a massive lack of education and expertise at the very bottom of our pathway.

'In this country, football’s everyone’s sport and everyone gets access to it but if you take your young kids along to swimming or gymnastics or whatever other sport, you’re there with properly qualified coaches, teachers who have maybe done things at the top levels of that sport.

'We have nothing without the volunteers that work at the bottom end of the grassroots game, but they’re not teaching kids how to play football properly, in my mind.

'And you’re just hoping that they get put with a volunteer coach that can teach them something, that they get in with a really good group at the youngest ages.

'Because that development between the ages of three and eight, if you don’t get that right, you’re dead. You can’t do anything after that point, and we don’t do it well enough. And you’re just chasing yourself and we’ll never catch up.'

Isaksen, Noslin e Taylor vogliono restare alla Lazio nonostante le attenzioni del mercato. Secondo quanto riportato da Corriere dello Sport, Gattuso ha parlato nelle ultime settimane con diversi giocatori per conoscerne gli umori e, finora, ha raccolto soltanto segnali favorevoli alla permanenza. Il tecnico spera che la sessione di gennaio non rompa il legame costruito con la squadra.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

Gattuso ha descritto così il rapporto nato nel gruppo: «Mi prendo un merito, aver fatto diventare questa squadra una famiglia». Nel parlare di famiglia ha richiamato anche il «senso di appartenenza» dei giocatori. È su questo legame che punta per trattenere i protagonisti della squadra, mentre cominciano a circolare le prime voci di mercato.

Il timore è quello di un nuovo ridimensionamento dopo le partenze iniziate un anno fa con Guendouzi e Castellanos e proseguite con altri giocatori, giustificate con la formula «hanno chiesto di andare via». Gattuso ha cercato di coinvolgere tutti, dando spazio anche a chi in precedenza si sentiva fuori posto. Il gruppo viene descritto attraverso i sorrisi, le parole e il clima tra i calciatori, ora al primo posto. Ai giocatori è attribuita una presa di posizione collettiva: «Siamo con Rino. Lottiamo per lui».

Nel rapporto con la squadra pesano anche la fame maturata dopo Zenica e l’idea di calcio affinata dal tecnico negli anni. Il modello della Lazio come gruppo familiare viene richiamato attraverso la storia del club, a partire dal primo scudetto. Sabato, all’Olimpico, i tifosi hanno cantato il nome di Gattuso.

Da giorni circolano interessamenti per gli olandesi Taylor e Noslin e per i danesi Isaksen e Provstgaard. Isaksen è stato tra i primi a esprimersi pubblicamente sul proprio futuro. Il Brentford di Matthew Benham lo corteggia: l’imprenditore britannico è noto per il lavoro svolto con Brentford e Midtjylland, il club nel quale l’esterno danese è emerso. Benham conosce bene il calcio danese.

Alla prospettiva della Premier League, Isaksen ha risposto: «La Premier? Voglio la Champions con la Lazio». Anche Provstgaard piace da tempo in Inghilterra. Taylor e Noslin possono attirare offerte, mentre Frattesi ha rifiutato alcune opzioni sia a gennaio sia a luglio.

Finora nessuno dei giocatori ha manifestato a Gattuso la volontà di partire. Da Taylor a Noslin, da Isaksen a Frattesi, prevale l’intenzione di proseguire il percorso con la Lazio. Le posizioni espresse dai calciatori chiamano la società a rispettare il patto costruito tra allenatore e squadra.

#2175. How Mykhailo Mudryk's unsolicited interview turned into a moany mess: With gaffes galore - including his Arsenal 'dream' - the £89m man hasn't endeared himself to either Spurs or Chelsea, writes KIERAN GILL

Published: 07:05 EST, 20 November 2026 | Updated: 11:02 EST, 20 November 2026

Not for the first time, Mykhailo Mudryk was in a good position, and displayed poor judgement. This time, it was away from the pitch, in a two-hour, sit-down, tell-all interview involving gaffes galore.

Criticising London for its 20mph speed limit, saying 'it's hard to follow these rules, however much I'd like to' after his latest driving ban, and how the wealthy are taxed 45 per cent on these shores.

Naming 'Ukraine' when asked which is the best city in the world – he may have misunderstood the question, so we will let him off with that one – and admitting he signed contracts without reading them while denouncing the deals done by his former representatives.

Then there was his interviewer, a grinning chap called Dmitry Gordon, asking him if he speaks to his four cats in English, and why he wears earrings, and if girls send him pictures. Bizarre enquiries. Had Chelsea or Tottenham Hotspur known about this interview happening – and Daily Mail Sport understands they were not informed – then we cannot imagine either Premier League club would have liked such lines of questioning.

Nor will they, or their fanbases, be too fond of the 25-year-old Ukrainian explaining his move to England in the first place. He revealed how much he wanted to play for Arsenal, describing it as his 'dream' as he blamed his old agent for pushing him to Chelsea for the money.

What was supposed to be an opportunity for Mudryk to tell his side of the story, after a doping ban which cost him two years of his career, resembled more of a moany mess.

Mykhailo Mudryk's interview was a bizarre mess and showed his utter naivety

The Ukrainian winger was given a one-year driving ban in December last year

By the end, the seriousness of his allegations against the Ukrainian national team with regard to his failed drugs test were softened by the nonsensical own goals much more likely to set social media alight.

Such as the naivety of a Chelsea player on loan at Tottenham appearing to pine for Arsenal, and saying he still wants to win the Ballon d'Or, which is only ever an admission prone to being patronised online even if a sign of his ambition.

A reminder: Mudryk signed for Chelsea from Shakhtar Donetsk in January 2023 for £89million. He struggled, never looked like a winger worth such a fee. Frighteningly fast, but did not know how to use his weaponry properly in the Premier League. Graham Potter was his manager when he arrived. A few months later, it was Frank Lampard. A month after that, Mauricio Pochettino.

Then he was suspended for two years, during which time Chelsea supported his insistence that he was innocent and continued to pay his salary, believed to be worth around £100,000 per week.

While banned, he turned up in Wroclaw, Poland the day before the 2025 Conference League final, apparently there to collect his medal in case Chelsea won. Enzo Maresca was unaware, only learning that he was in the city when we told him at his pre-match press conference.

Upon Mudryk's return to football in the summer of 2026, Chelsea decided he needed to leave on loan, and another one of the Premier League's big-six sides gave him his second chance.

Tottenham called. Brentford, Brighton, Leeds and Coventry were among those also willing, but Roberto De Zerbi promised to give him a platform to prove his talents.

He appeared once, as a 74th-minute substitute away at Nottingham Forest in September. Then he disappeared, injured, and his only sighting since has been here, on YouTube, as a 'Guest of Gordon'.

Chelsea will also be unhappy about Mudryk's interview and were not informed about it

Mykhailo Mudryk's is still two weeks away from a Tottenham return after a suffering setback to his recovery from an ankle injury.

Mudryk, who has played only 16 minutes since joining on loan from Chelsea, clashed with the Ukrainian FA this week when he blamed them for his positive drug test which ruled him out of football for almost two years.

He also risked the wrath of Spurs fans by admitting he only made the move across London to recover his fitness before returning to Stamford Bridge where Blues boss Xabi Alonso was 'counting on him'.

And he managed to annoy both Chelsea and Spurs fans by revealing, during the same YouTube interview, that he wanted to sign for Arsenal when he moved to the Premier League in January 2023 but his agent forced through the move with the best commission.

'I can understand the fans, but Mudryk is a good guy,' said Spurs boss De Zerbi, who worked with the Ukraine international at Shakhtar Dontetsk and convinced him to resist interest from Brentford and Coventry and be reunited in North London.

'He's a good player. He suffered a lot for the situation in the last couple of years. He has just one thing to do, show on the pitch his qualities, show on the pitch that now he's playing for Tottenham. Tottenham is a big club, like the other clubs, not less at all.'

Chelsea paid £86million for Mudryk but he made little impression before he was hit with a four-year ban in 2024, which was wiped out this summer enabling him to return to competition.

'He has no responsibility in this part,' said De Zerbi. 'Now his focus is just on the ball or just on the football. He has a big chance to play with us because Tottenham is a big club. He knows very well the coach, he knows very well the style of play.

'He knows I give him total confidence to show what he is. So to move the focus in the right direction, in a good direction. But I think he's working hard to recover and to play earlier.'

Mudryk made his Spurs debut as a sub at Nottingham Forest in September but injured an ankle in training a few days later. He was initially expected to be out for two months with Saturday's game against Ipswich his target to return.

'The plan was to be available for Ipswich or Sunderland,' said De Zerbi, who will serve a touchline ban against Ipswich for three yellow cards. 'Then he felt not pain but he was not comfortable last week. And they take two weeks more, and step by step we're going to see the situation.'

While there should be sympathy with Mudryk, who had to bite his tongue while banned and spending what he described as 'millions' on trying to clear his name, he made a poor choice in the platform chosen to finally speak up.

At one stage, he is asked bluntly whether he considered killing himself. 'No, that's not a way out,' he replied. The title of the video on YouTube, unhelpfully, mentions 'suicidal thoughts' regardless of his answer.

Mudryk may have thought it would help to talk so openly, especially to clarify those he holds responsible for the two years he lost as a footballer.

But he hardly endeared himself to his current club, Tottenham, or to his parent club, Chelsea, to whom he is still contracted into the 2030s.

He likened them to chaos, explaining: 'I came to a Chelsea that was, and perhaps still is, in the middle of a rebuild. It's not the Chelsea that existed under the previous owners, which was stable, had a clear system, coaches, without this constant galloping who knows where.'

Such comments are unlikely to be well received when read by the club's hierarchy, while he also said on Chelsea's managerial merry-go-round: 'The coaches themselves don't know what tactics they'll play. They come to the club and try one set of tactics. Whether it works or not, they try another.'

When Mudryk returns from his ankle injury, he will have to hope his football can do the talking. After this, neither Tottenham nor Chelsea are likely to want him to speak again any time soon.

#2172. Totti a Belve, in difficoltà con gli acronimi: "LGBTQIA+? Non sono tecnologico"

Nell’anticipazione dell’intervista, Francesca Fagnani suggerisce la prima parola della sigla latina.

Francesco Totti è alle prese con gli acronimi SPQR e LGBTQIA+ in un’anticipazione della sua intervista a Belve. L’ex capitano della Roma fatica a ricordare il significato della prima sigla e risponde con alcune battute alle domande di Francesca Fagnani sulla seconda. Lo scambio è riportato da Cinemaserietv.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

Tra le prime domande dell’anteprima c’è quella su SPQR, l’acronimo latino che compare da secoli sullo stemma di Roma e che per anni è stato presente anche sulle maglie giallorosse. Totti non sembra ricordarne con precisione il significato: «SPQR erano quelle… che era… ‘na cosa che nun me ricordo bene».

Fagnani gli suggerisce la prima parola, Senatus. L’ex numero dieci prova a proseguire, poi si ferma: «Senatus? Mo’ pure il latino vuoi… Senatus Popolusco e Romanus so’ troppe frasi».

La conduttrice passa quindi a LGBTQIA+. Totti sembra non riconoscere l’acronimo e, inizialmente, lo associa alla tecnologia: «LGBT? Nun so’ tecnologico io. LGBTQ Plus. Ma che è, un telefonino?».

Fagnani insiste con la domanda e l’ex calciatore continua sullo stesso tono ironico: «18 Plus? Ma ndo’ le tiri fuori ‘ste domande?. Ma come famo?». Il botta e risposta fa parte delle anticipazioni dell’intervista che Totti concederà alla conduttrice.

#2168. Benitez, la sentenza sull'Inter di Chivu: “È pazzerella. Ecco qual è il suo tormento”

Benitez, la sentenza sull'Inter di Chivu: “È pazzerella. Ecco qual è il suo tormento”

Installa la nostra App gratuita e accedi più velocemente alle notizie

Mentre navighi nell'app, scorri il dito da sinistra a destra dello schermo per tornare alle pagine precedenti

Scegli Fcinter1908.it come tuo sito preferito su Google: clicca qui

Intervistato da La Gazzetta dello Sport, l’ex tecnico dell’Inter Rafa Benitez ha analizzato il momento della squadra di Chivu dopo il pareggio di Bergamo e in vista della sfida di domani sera contro lo Stoccarda.

«Sempre meno, perché ormai ne restano sempre meno di partite. Se sei in ritardo, rischi di compromettere il passaggio del turno; se sei in vantaggio, come il Como, conviene metterti in sicurezza e tentare di entrare tra le prime otto. Si può fare».

«La Roma a Parigi sfida non solo i campioni in carica ma anche una delle squadre più in salute. Ma c’è una domanda da porsi: cosa perderà Luis Enrique senza Kvaratskhelia e Doué? La struttura resta imponente, ci mancherebbe, ma queste sono assenze egualmente penalizzanti. E l’ultima partita di Gasperini, a Parma, ha rappresentato un segnale per chiunque: la squadra sta bene, segna e ha un centravanti pazzesco, che fa paura, come Malen. Sono argomenti anche questi».

«Si resta spiazzati dinnanzi a certe serate: avanti di due, sempre in controllo, con un attaccante come Pio Esposito che pure in Nazionale sta facendo cose enormi, subisce la reazione dell’Atalanta, che ha meriti grossi ci mancherebbe, e butta via una vittoria che pareva blindata. È un’Inter pazzerella, come suol dirsi: prima andava sotto e rimontava; ora scappa e si fa riprendere. Segna tantissimo ma subisce altrettanto e immagino che questo rappresenti il tormento di Chivu, di cui ho letto le riflessioni: riuscire a trovare l’equilibrio che manca è la sua missione, lo comprendo».

«L’Inter fa appena in tempo a ritrovare Lautaro Martinez e perde Calhanoglu. È chiaro che sono preoccupazioni anche queste, che si aggiungono a quelle del calendario. Lo Stoccarda, come qualsiasi tedesca di rango, è un’insidia e queste sono partite-trappola. Poi ci saranno la trasferta a Dortmund, mai facile, e il Liverpool a San Siro. Non è consentito rallentare, non proprio ora».

Un 4-4 come quello di Bergamo lascia scorie?

«Si fa in fretta a dimenticarlo, anzi si deve. E in questo sarà d’aiuto la personalità di Chivu. Certe fasi negative vanno risolte con le soluzioni che si trovano nello spogliatoio e con il lavoro. Diciassette reti subite in campionato rappresentano il tema dominante delle analisi dell’allenatore, che essendo stato un difensore di portata internazionale ci metterà le mani. Il primo ad avere un quadro limpido di questa Inter è lui, fidatevi».

#2166. Pocognoli's problems are just the tip of the iceberg when it comes to those running the show at the SFA

Published: 10:30 EST, 20 November 2026 | Updated: 11:25 EST, 20 November 2026

For anyone with anything resembling a retentive memory, the alarm bells were going off all over the shop on the day Sebastien Pocognoli was unveiled as the new Scotland head coach.

Scottish Football Association (SFA) chief executive Ian Maxwell and chief football officer Craig Mulholland were there to crow about an extensive search of foreign fields to rival Captain Cook’s journeys around the Pacific and how data had helped land ‘one of Europe’s most sought-after coaches’ and secure the ‘aggressive, front-foot style of play’ that was going to bring a desired freshness.

It was impossible to avoid the elephant in the room, though. Two-and-a-half months earlier, ahead of the disaster zone that was the World Cup, they’d handed Steve Clarke a fresh four-year contract.

He was the polar opposite to Poco. Aggressive and front-foot, he most certainly wasn’t. The SFA knew Clarke didn’t have it in him to go for it at a major finals – as evidenced by two separate European Championship failures – but they decided to stick with the status quo anyway. Just as they did after Euro 2024, when he should have been sacked.

When Clarke finally did go of his own accord after the summer’s events in North America, Maxwell stated he was ‘sad’ the former Kilmarnock manager had stepped down and put a big dollop of blame on ‘some fairly hysterical commentary back home’.

SFA chief football officer Craig Mulholland,  Pocognoli and SFA chief executive Ian Maxwell

It just didn’t add up. How do you go from thinking a guy like Clarke is the future to tearing everything up, drafting in a London-based analytics firm to tell you who to go for as his replacement and coming up with a 39-year-old Belgian who wants to go for broke?

‘It doesn’t matter how you get there,’ offered Maxwell. ‘Let’s not get too hung up on the whys and the what-ifs.’

Unfortunately, people are now starting to get hung up over the fact Pocognoli, for all the good on his CV, looks like he’s in the wrong movie – with his refusal to break from a failing three-at-the-back strategy suggesting a diploma from the Wilfried Nancy School of Intransigence ought to be on that resume too.

They are getting hung up on the fact captain Andy Robertson came out after the thrashing from Switzerland’s B-team to reveal the mood inside the camp has changed and warn there’s no breathing space to sort this out.

And they are getting hung up on the fact that it’s becoming considerably easier to reach the conclusion those above all of them at the very top of the SFA have never had a proper grip on things at all.

Clarke, of course, only exacerbated those concerns in September by expressing the view at a public event that Maxwell and Co ‘panicked a little bit’ in giving him that incomprehensible long-term deal because he’d threatened to announce he was quitting before the finals unless an agreement was reached.

‘I wanted to control my narrative,’ he explained. It was hard to know who emerged from those admissions looking worse.

Clarke’s off the plot now, though, and it’s Maxwell, Mulholland and the SFA president Mike Mulraney left to try and convince us all that there is some kind of rhyme or reason to it all.

How things shape up with Pocognoli is an acid test for them. The rise or fall of a Scotland manager always hogs headlines. Yet, it is only one of a number of things coming together in what is starting to feel like a bit of a perfect storm.

Not so long ago, Maxwell and Mulholland were also yakking plenty about their statement project to increase game time for Scotland-eligible Under-21s.

It’s already in tatters, by the looks of things. The plans were watered down last month, without anything like the same fanfare, to involve Under-23 players and the intended votes on the proposals were pushed back.

A number of Premiership sides haven’t warmed to it and it remains to be seen whether it goes to a vote at all. Certainly, the radical change the top brass envisioned isn’t going to happen and that can only be regarded as an indicator that these guys running the game aren’t capable of taking it with them.

The start of Sebastien Pocognoli's reign has been concerning for Scotland fans

Dundee manager Steven Pressley’s intervention over recent days hasn’t helped them either. After a long period on the outside looking in, Scotland have turned out in three major tournaments in the last five years.

That has raised many millions of pounds, but the effect on elite level is questionable. Pressley talked of the sport being ‘built on sand’ as a result of there being no kind of national facility and you’d better believe it gained traction.

‘It says a lot about our strategy, our longevity and our long-term vision and we are finally seeing the effects of that. We have shown none of that over the period and it is all horribly catching up with us,’ he stated.

The SFA do own Hampden, of course, thanks to that peculiar £5million deal from 2019 that involved Willie Haughey and Sir Tom Hunter ponying up half the money. Yet, how distant those days of Maxwell talking about taking examples from the way VfB Stuttgart revamped the Mercedes-Benz Arena and potentially implementing them in Mount Florida seem now.

Everyone appears to have given up on making the National Stadium better. Other problems have overtaken that old chestnut. Refereeing isn’t good enough. VAR isn’t up to scratch. General facilities require considerable improvement.

Should this Youth Plan fail, there may also be a need for the SFA to look deeper within when addressing the dearth of top-tier talent coming through.

It’s OK to blame clubs, but what about the level of coaching – and coach education – that exists at the national association? That’s before we even get to the ‘massive lack of expertise and education’ at the younger end of grassroots raised by St Mirren boss Craig McLeish.

The SFA set-up for producing coaches was once the envy of many. We all remember Jose Mourinho and others at the Largs finishing school. Now? Even Maxwell, after stating there were no viable Scottish rivals to Pocognoli for the Scotland job, admitted the coaching pathway needs looked at.

There is only one problem here, though. Maxwell has been the chief executive since May 2018. He’s in his ninth year in the role. What has he really achieved? What have his big ideas been?

Yes, Scotland made it back to major finals under Clarke, but Clarke was a painfully obvious choice to be made national coach in 2019. He’d worked wonders at Rugby Park and did transfer that to the international scene until it came to actually turning out in those showpiece competitions.

Now they’ve had to get creative, there is so much hanging on Pocognoli’s reign for the guys at the top.

Maxwell was a bloke who landed in the role of Partick Thistle GM almost by mistake after failing to beat Jackie McNamara to the manager’s job. Just before leaving for the SFA, the Jags got relegated.

Talk of redeveloping Hampden has gone quiet as other problems take the spotlight

At his Hampden unveiling, he had to fend off questions about only getting that position through behind-the-scenes politicking and insist he wouldn’t be a puppet for anyone.

Mulholland also seems a surprising choice as the architect of the national sport’s renovation. He spent two decades in the Rangers academy and eight years as head of it.

Has it been a conveyor belt of talent for the Ibrox first-team? Do you recall much wailing and gnashing of teeth when he left in 2023 amid a wider changing of the guard?

Then, there’s Mulraney, the former Alloa chairman and SFA director for over a decade.

You don’t see much of him these days. Maybe because people want to ask about his position as chair of FIFA’s finance committee in the wake of the most greed-infested World Cup ever and the ongoing soap opera surrounding Gianni Infantino. But maybe not.

How things work out with Pocognoli might end up being the one thing that puts all these guys under the kind of examination that cannot be bodyswerved, but it’s only the tip of the iceberg.

#2164. Inter-Stoccarda, pronostico marcatori: le quote sui possibili marcatori

Inter-Stoccarda, pronostico marcatori: le quote sui possibili marcatori

Installa la nostra App gratuita e accedi più velocemente alle notizie

Mentre navighi nell'app, scorri il dito da sinistra a destra dello schermo per tornare alle pagine precedenti

La Champions League torna protagonista a San Siro, dove mercoledì 25 novembre, alle ore 21, l'Inter di Cristian Chivu ospiterà lo Stoccarda nella quinta giornata della League Phase. I nerazzurri arrivano all'appuntamento dopo il rocambolesco 4-4 sul campo dell'Atalanta, una partita che ha confermato il potenziale offensivo della squadra, ma anche alcune difficoltà nella gestione delle situazioni difensive. In Europa l'Inter cerca invece la terza vittoria consecutiva, dopo i successi contro Shakhtar Donetsk e Feyenoord, per rilanciare le proprie ambizioni di qualificazione diretta agli ottavi. Riflettori puntati soprattutto sulle scelte offensive di Chivu, chiamato a gestire il rientro di Lautaro Martínez e le condizioni di Marcus Thuram, con Francesco Pio Esposito pronto a giocarsi una maglia da titolare. Tre soluzioni differenti per caratteristiche, ma tutte potenzialmente importanti contro una formazione tedesca che tende a giocare con coraggio e a lasciare spazi nelle transizioni. Sul fronte opposto, attenzione soprattutto a Deniz Undav ed Ermedin Demirović, principali riferimenti offensivi della squadra di Sebastian Hoeneß, senza dimenticare la qualità di Bilal El Khannouss sulla trequarti. Anche gli inserimenti dei centrocampisti e le situazioni da palla inattiva potrebbero offrire occasioni ai protagonisti meno attesi.

Se sei un nuovo utente per confrontare in maniera più completa le quote può essere utile analizzare anche l'eventuale bonus applicato dagli stessi operatori sui nuovi iscritti.

Tra i possibili protagonisti della notte europea di San Siro c'è Francesco Pio Esposito, pronto a ritagliarsi un ruolo importante nell'attacco dell'Inter di Cristian Chivu. Il giovane centravanti nerazzurro rappresenta una soluzione particolarmente interessante per le sue caratteristiche fisiche, la capacità di proteggere il pallone e la pericolosità all'interno dell'area di rigore. Contro uno Stoccarda che predilige un calcio propositivo e tende a lasciare spazi agli avversari, Esposito potrebbe trovare occasioni per sfruttare i rifornimenti provenienti dalle corsie esterne. Il gol di Pio Esposito viene proposto a quota 1.70 da Planetwin365, 1.82 da StarVegas, 1.85 da Eurobet e 1.94 da StarCasinò. Un confronto che evidenzia valutazioni differenti tra gli operatori, con StarCasinò che presenta la quota più alta tra quelle analizzate.

Se sei un nuovo utente per confrontare in maniera più completa le quote può essere utile analizzare anche l'eventuale bonus applicato dagli stessi operatori sui nuovi iscritti.

Una possibilità meno scontata nel mercato dei marcatori riguarda Federico Dimarco, uno dei giocatori più importanti nello sviluppo offensivo dell'Inter. L'esterno nerazzurro rappresenta una risorsa preziosa per la qualità dei cross, la precisione sui calci piazzati e la capacità di rendersi pericoloso anche con conclusioni dalla distanza. In una partita nella quale lo Stoccarda potrebbe concedere spazi sulle corsie laterali, Dimarco potrebbe avere l'opportunità di accompagnare l'azione e cercare direttamente la porta avversaria. La sua eventuale rete rappresenta un'ipotesi meno frequente rispetto a quella di un attaccante, ma trova comunque spazio nelle valutazioni dei bookmaker. Il gol di Dimarco è quotato 3.85 da Planetwin365, 3.95 da StarVegas, 4.20 da Eurobet e 4.60 da StarCasinò. Anche in questo caso è StarCasinò a proporre la quota più elevata, con una differenza significativa rispetto agli altri operatori.

#2163. Taylor resta alla Lazio a gennaio: a febbraio premio fedeltà da 190mila euro

Dopo le rassicurazioni a Gattuso, il club valuta un ritocco dell’ingaggio: prima affronterà i contratti in scadenza nel 2028.

Kenneth Taylor vuole restare alla Lazio a gennaio e il club pensa a un ritocco del suo ingaggio. Secondo quanto riportato da Il Messaggero, il centrocampista ha rassicurato Gattuso dopo l’interesse arrivato dalla Premier League e dalla Juventus. La società intende occuparsi prima dei contratti in scadenza nel 2028, poi affrontare anche la sua situazione.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

Durante la sosta della scorsa settimana a Formello, Rino era in allarme per le sirene del mercato. Sono bastate poche parole di Taylor per tranquillizzarlo. Il giocatore gli ha confermato l’interesse ricevuto: «È tutto vero, i miei agenti mi hanno informato.» Gli ha però chiarito che non intende partire a gennaio: «Ma ho già detto che a gennaio non ci penso proprio ad andare via.»

Taylor ha espresso a Gattuso anche il suo obiettivo: «Voglio restare qui e aiutare la Lazio a tornare in Champions». Il tecnico non si aspettava questa risposta e ne è stato molto felice. Isaksen e Provstgaard gli avevano già promesso fedeltà e appartenenza. A 24 anni, Taylor è descritto come un giocatore già affermato, pronto per un salto di livello e capace di trovare posto in molte grandi squadre italiane e non solo.

Anche Fabiani e Lotito vogliono trattenere Taylor. Se il suo percorso proseguirà così, a giugno potrebbero non bastare neppure 40 milioni di euro per convincerli a trattare con Juventus, Brentford e Bournemouth. La Lazio vuole ricompensarlo per l’attaccamento mostrato e per aver rinunciato a guadagni più alti offerti altrove.

Dopo aver affrontato i contratti in scadenza nel 2028, il club parlerà di un possibile adeguamento per Kenneth, che percepisce 1,7 milioni di euro fino al 2030. A febbraio riceverà il secondo premio fedeltà da 190mila euro, dopo aver scelto per la seconda volta di restare in biancoceleste. Acquistato dall’Ajax per meno di 17 milioni di euro, con il club olandese che conserva il 10% su una futura rivendita, Taylor si è rivelato un colpo straordinario per la Lazio.

#2160. Man United CEO Omar Berrada insists the team have IMPROVED since the transfer window in his first interview in five months - but does accept criticism of leadership has been fair

Published: 10:25 EST, 20 November 2026 | Updated: 11:32 EST, 20 November 2026

Manchester United CEO Omar Berrada insists the team got 'fundamentally better' after this summer's transfer window, as he warned ‘spending just for the sake of it’ is not the route to putting United back on top.

In his first sit-down interview in five months, Berrada tackled topics ranging from the relationship between the Glazers and Ineos, the imminent departure of youngster JJ Gabriel, the decision not to sign a left back in the summer, as well as the decision to push ahead for a new stadium despite supporter backlash on matchdays.

The United chief has been at the centre of furious protests and anti-ownership chanting at matches this season, alongside Director of Football Jason Wilcox, and he accepted that criticism of United's leadership has, mostly, been fair.

In an interview that aired shortly after new head coach Mauricio Pochettino spoke to the media for the first time, Berrada was pressed on how much accountability falls at his door with United heading to Anfield this weekend 18th in the Premier League table.

'I think if you think about the fact we've had three permanent managers in less than three seasons, clearly we haven't achieved the stability that we wanted, that's for sure,' Berrada told Sky Sports.

'When it doesn't work out with the coach or the manager, we are all responsible. Myself, the football leadership, everyone who is involved around the coach and the staff and the players. We have to continue improving and getting better.

Manchester United's CEO Omar Berrada finally fronted up in an interview with Sky Sports

Michael Carrick's sacking was yet another managerial change at United, with the hierarchy under massive scrutiny and Mauricio Pochettino now in the hotseat

'There are different circumstances why it didn't work out with Erik [ten Hag], and Ruben [Amorim], and now Michael [Carrick], and we are always learning and, as I said, trying to improve and understand what has failed.

'The challenge is we're trying to find the right manager for the squad that we have with a specific set of skills in that moment to make them perform at their best level.

'It's true, we have to accept the criticism that we haven't been able to achieve that stability with one coach and our aim is to do that as soon as possible. Of course, we hope now that Mauricio coming in will give us that stability that I think we all need.'

Berrada's links to Manchester City, where he worked prior to joining United, were brought up, not least given he worked at the club during the period that City have had charges brought against them.

But at his new home there are issues aplenty, not least having got another managerial appointment wrong, sacking Carrick less than six months after handing him a two-year deal, and so it was put to Berrada: is the club actually progressing?

'It's not good enough, not acceptable and that's why we've made the change that we made so that we can move forward,' he concluded.

(L-R) Sir Jim Ratcliffe, Berrada and Jason Wilcox have drawn plenty of ire from Man United fans

'But we are laying the foundations for sustained growth, to put us in a position where we can be competitive consistently. It's not always been that.

'Last year we finished third, we were happy with the squad, we were happy with the way we were going. And now, a few months later, we are not where we need to be. But the foundations are there and I am convinced that we are heading in the right direction. Very positive.'

Caso arbitri e bussate in sala VAR: archiviata l'inchiesta dal tribunale di Monza

Installa la nostra App gratuita e accedi più velocemente alle notizie

"La giudice per le indagini preliminari del Tribunale di Monza Letizia Anna Brambilla ha archiviato l'inchiesta per concorso in frode sportiva sulle presunte “bussate” nella sala Var di Lissone tra il 2024 e il 2025". La giudice ha accolto in toto le argomentazioni della Procura di Monza, che aveva chiesto di non procedere nei confronti del designatore arbitrale e supervisore Gianluca Rocchi, del supervisore degli ufficiali di gara addetti al Var Andrea Gervasoni e degli arbitri addetti al Var Luigi Nasca e Rodolfo Di Vuolo.

Scegli Fcinter1908.it come tuo sito preferito su Google: clicca qui

Si legge sul sito del quotidiano Il Giorno che per primo aveva parlato anche delle richieste di archiviazioni del Tribunale di Milano. Nasca e Di Vuolo erano indagati per la mancata raccomandazione di una on field review nell'azione che precedette la rete del 2-1". Rocchi era indagato per le famose bussate durante Udinese-Parma e Gervasoni per un presunto condizionamento in Salernitana-Modena.

Santini e Gittardi, i due pm che si sono occupati della vicenda hanno evidenzato, da come riporta il quotidiano: "criticità istituzionali e organizzative nel settore arbitrale, segnato da tensioni interne, contrapposizioni sulla governance, perplessità sulla regolamentazione della sala Var e rischi derivanti dalla promiscuità dei rapporti tra organi arbitrali, federazione e società calcistiche. Profili che possono - al limite ed eventualmente - assumere rilievo in altre sedi, disciplinare, regolamentare o politico-sportivo, ma non risultano sufficienti a fondare l'esercizio dell'azione penale per frode sportiva".

#2157. Bussate, Gip archivia: distrutto caso Doveri-Inter. "Fondazione Jdentità bianconera nulla può pretendere"

Installa la nostra App gratuita e accedi più velocemente alle notizie

Mentre navighi nell'app, scorri il dito da sinistra a destra dello schermo per tornare alle pagine precedenti

Nessun vantaggio personale o "delle squadre coinvolte"; piuttosto è stata fatta "la scelta più consapevole, nella valutazione allargata dei nomi tecnicamente possibili, la scelta più adeguata ad evitare errori in gara".

#2156. McInnes wants a quiet January... but it might just be the right time for Rangers to cash in on Raskin

Published: 11:37 EST, 20 November 2026 | Updated: 11:44 EST, 20 November 2026

Derek McInnes can be understood in wanting a quiet January window. He’s still bonding a squad of players together. Drama and diversions are the last thing he needs when there looks to be an opportunity to win a title on the horizon.

In the case of Olwethu Makhanya, for example, this is definitely not the time to sell.

The 22-year-old South African has been impressive at centre-back, but it will take a campaign catching the attention in high-level European football for him to raise his value to Calvin Bassey levels and beyond.

Same goes for the likes of Ryan Naderi. Getting into the Czechia squad and increasing his range of experience makes him a more interesting prospect, but UEFA competition is a platform that ought to increase his premium.

McInnes, as he did this week, has to say he wants to keep his more prominent talents anyway. It would be strange to appear accepting of the board cashing in on bigger players.

When it comes to Nico Raskin, however, things are different. A buyer for him should have been found in the summer after his World Cup involvement with Belgium.

Rangers midfielder Nico Raskin is an asset who could be sold in the January transfer window

From the chit-chat around the game, it does not seem unreasonable to think Rangers maybe overvalued him in the initial part of that window and missed the boat in terms of securing a sale.

If he stays for the rest of this season and enters the final year of his contract, though, the money you can ask for naturally reduces. The club might seek to tie him up on a new deal, of course, but his remarks about being ‘mentally ready to leave’ at the start of the season point to the most likely direction of travel.

Is he irreplaceable? Not at all. And this is where forward planning should come into the mix.

If there’s someone out there willing to edge anywhere close to the £15m mark for the 25-year-old, they should be getting sounded out now – with a contingency plan being discussed and worked on in tandem.

Yes, Rangers need to get back to winning silverware, but the need to start raising funds through selling players – and replacing them - at appropriate times is pressing too.

As often said here, they can’t keep spending money the way they have been without considerably more coming back in return.

Raskin’s a good player, but his three-and-a-half years at Ibrox have hardly been shaped by his propensity for winning medals, have they? If a realistic fee is there in January, there’s a case to make for it being taken.

#2151. Lazio, Gattuso come Eriksson per il clima: Frattesi accostato a Nedved

Belahyane viene paragonato ad Almeyda, mentre Mandas richiama Marchegiani per le qualità tra i pali e nelle uscite.

La Lazio di Gattuso ha raccolto nelle prime dodici giornate più punti di quella del 2000, considerata tra le più forti della storia biancoceleste per l’organico al completo. Secondo quanto riportato da Il Messaggero, Da quando la vittoria vale tre punti, soltanto la Juventus, prima con Capello e poi con Allegri, ha fatto meglio. È da questi numeri che Il Messaggero parte per accostare le due squadre, pur tenendo distinte le rispettive epoche.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

Rino, Frattesi e gli altri biancocelesti continuano a guardare al traguardo dei 40 punti, indicato come quota della salvezza matematica. Dopo la vittoria sul Lecce, il tecnico calabrese aveva spiegato: «E dopo parleremo di altro, con maggiore tranquillità».

Il confronto con la squadra del secondo scudetto riguarda anche lo spogliatoio. Gattuso vi ha portato un clima che richiama, con le dovute proporzioni, quello creato da Eriksson ventisei anni fa. Nella Lazio di allora, però, l’armonia non era quella di oggi: convivevano il gruppo degli ex Samp, guidato da Roberto Mancini, e quello degli argentini, senza un capo designato ma rappresentato dalle personalità di Veron e Simeone.

Il paragone suggerito dalla partita contro il Lecce è quello tra Belahyane e Almeyda. Il marocchino intercetta palloni e li sottrae agli avversari commettendo raramente fallo, come faceva l’argentino. Almeyda era chiamato Capitan Uncino per il modo in cui usava il piede destro per portare via il pallone; Belahyane si affida invece alla rapidità della corsa e all’appoggio del corpo sul rivale per interrompere l’azione.

Tra i portieri, Mandas richiama Luca Marchegiani: entrambi efficaci tra i pali, dotati di personalità sulle palle alte e meno talentuosi nel gioco con i piedi. A Marchegiani costò il posto in Nazionale un errore che Sacchi non gli perdonò.

A centrocampo, Frattesi ricorda Nedved quando arriva nei pressi dell’area pronto a colpire. Il ceco era un attaccante aggiunto per Zoff ed Eriksson; Davide è l’incursore della squadra di Gattuso. Alcuni movimenti di Taylor richiamano Stankovic, più potente soprattutto nel tiro dalla distanza. L’olandese viene accostato anche a Simeone per la capacità di contribuire con gol e assist senza dare nell’occhio.

#2150. Man United chief Omar Berrada distances himself from Man City's Premier League charges - as Enzo Maresca insists his stars are unaffected by guilty verdict

By NATHAN SALT - FOOTBALL REPORTER and JACK GAUGHAN

Published: 12:41 EST, 20 November 2026 | Updated: 12:46 EST, 20 November 2026

Manchester United chief executive Omar Berrada has insisted he was not involved in any wrongdoing at his former club Manchester City – but refused to rule out taking action against them if their appeal fails.

In his first sit-down interview in five months, broadcast on Friday, Berrada tackled a range of topics concerning United but his links to City, from whom he joined United in 2024, were also brought up. He worked at the club during some of the period that City were found to have broken Premier League rules.

Back in September, an independent regulatory commission found City guilty of a multitude of charges brought against them by the Premier League, with financial offences ranging between 2009 and 2018.

The scathing verdict determined that City had 'artificially inflated' their revenues and reduced costs through 'disguised funding' schemes such as 'sham' contracts.

Berrada joined City in 2011 before going on to become their chief operating officer in 2016, raising questions over whether he could end up implicated if City – who vehemently contest the charges – fail in their appeal.

'I'm not concerned,' Berrada insisted. 'I want to be very clear about this, right. I wasn't involved in any wrongdoings. I wasn't involved in the proceedings; there are no allegations against me, and in that sense there will be no surprises.'

Asked if United would push for compensation should City's guilty verdict be upheld on appeal, Berrada remained tight-lipped.

Omar Berrada joined Manchester City in 2011... and moved across the city 13 years later

Berrada has become one of the main sources of anger among United fans who have campaigned against the owners and the board

'I don't want to comment on that because right now it's an ongoing process,' he added. 'When it runs its course we'll take our view of where is our position as Manchester United.'

Berrada was not used as a witness in the original investigation and there are no allegations directed specifically to the now-United CEO.

City boss Enzo Maresca, meanwhile, insisted that the guilty verdicts handed down to Manchester City for financial chicanery have not affected his players despite three consecutive Premier League defeats.

City fell six points behind defending champions Arsenal in the weeks before this month’s international break amid a slew of individual high-profile errors that led to claims of Maresca’s squad being preoccupied by the uncertainty surrounding the club.

The Premier League have a range of available sanctions to them were City to lose an appeal set for next month. Those would likely result in relegation to the Championship.

But Maresca does not equate that looming doomsday backdrop with a worrying run of form before they face Fulham at the Etihad Stadium on Saturday lunchtime.

‘I don't think the players are thinking too much about that,’ Maresca said. ‘If we want to relate the noise with the defeats, look at [Aston] Villa away. We had so many chances, for instance Iliman [Ndiaye] hit the post, or Brighton where the keeper was man of the match.

Enzo Maresca insists his stars are not thinking about the impact of the guilty verdict

Erling Haaland says he is 'incredibly' happy to be a City player and tops the Premier League scoring charts with nine so far this campaign

‘Then [Nottingham] Forest when Erling [Haaland] had the chance to score for 2-2. I don't think the players are focused on the noise. I don't think they are worried about that.’

Haaland claimed to be ‘incredibly happy’ about being a City player in a defiant interview with Norwegian television earlier in the week, although few others have addressed the problems publicly.

Marc Guehi has previously said the players are ‘relaxed’, while Phil Foden refused to discuss it when asked.

Maresca has spent the past fortnight analysing City’s ten league matches and has admitted that the team requires more balance after conceding too many goals to turnovers or counter attacks.

‘I was not thinking that we were doing everything well when we were winning games and I'm not thinking that we did everything wrong when we lost games,’ the Italian added.

‘For me it is the beginning of something long. I think we are attacking well because we are creating chances and overall we are controlling games.

‘There are moments we are not controlling and we have to realise the reasons why and do better. We are conceding goals and most are avoidable from my point of view.’

There have been occasions – like the first goal at Forest – when City’s building from the back has put them in serious trouble. Josko Gvardiol lost the ball at the City Ground after no midfielder had shown for possession.

Maresca said: ‘We reviewed things during the break. The idea is to continue to attack in the same way and try to find the balance when we defend or when we are building like what happened for the first goal against Forest.

‘When you are building and you lose the ball in the build-up it's difficult to build with that.’

#2147. Europa League, ecco quanti punti servono ai rossoneri per qualificarsi ai play-off: e giovedì c’è la bolgia di Atene

Congelato momentaneamente il campionato dopo l'ultima vittoria casalinga per 5-1 contro il Frosinone, per i rossoneri è tempo di concentrarsi sull'Europa League. La classifica è di quelle veramente brutte da guardare: il Milan si trova alla 29° posizione con soli 3 punti conquistati al giro di boa della League Phase. La situazione è negativa, sì, ma non irrecuperabile. A quattro giornate dalla fine appare ormai molto difficile l'accesso diretto agli ottavi di finale, al contrario invece i play-off rimangono ampiamente alla portata.

Ma quanti punti servono al club meneghino per giocarsi l'accesso agli ottavi di finale nel doppio confronto dei play-off? Considerando la classifica attuale, i rossoneri potrebbero entrare nelle prime 24 posizionil con 9 punti. Attualmente l'ultima posizione utile è occupata dallo Sturm Graz (24°) che insieme a Hoffenheim (23°) e Sparta Praga (22°) sono a 4 punti. 9 punti però, potrebbero anche non bastare in caso di determinate combinazioni quindi, per stare sicuri, ai rossoneri servono ancora due vittorie ed un pareggio nelle prossime 4 per non rischiare un primo grosso fallimento stagionale.

Sebastian Hoeness, allenatore dello Stoccarda, ha parlato in conferenza stampa alla vigilia dell'impegno di Champions League contro l'Inter. Qui le sue considerazioni: "Stiller? So che vorreste la formazione, lui ha recuperato ed è in grado di giocare, vale per tutti gli altri. Dobbiamo capire che se avessimo per caso segnato un gol l'impressione dei media sarebbe stata diversa. Veniamo da uno 0-0 e di sicuro vorremmo vincere di più e non perdere ancora tanti punti per strada. Abbiamo anche da pensare alla Champions League, ma avremmo dovuto avere almeno due punti in più in Europa se pensiamo a quanto fatto in casa dell'Atletico. La sfortuna ci ha accompagnato nel percorso, speriamo di riuscire a fare meglio domani".

Undav non ha brillato contro lo Schalke. "Non è solamente lui il tema, sono tutti sempre in discussione. Che lui polarizzi lo sappiamo, ma non voglio parlare di un singolo. Non abbiamo segnato contro lo Schalke, ma è una responsabilità che riguarda tutti".

Che impressione ti fa questo stadio?"Siamo contentissimi di essere qui, abbiamo fatto un giro nello stadio ed è stato impressionante. E' alto, enorme, come lo ricordavo quando l'ho visto da spettatore da adolescente. Guardo tante partite in tv, ho visto anche il derby. Questo stadio ha una storia pazzesca, incluso il capitolo dei mondiali del '90. Siamo felici di poter giocare qui ed essere parte della storia di questo stadio. E' fantastico anche senza tifosi, non vediamo l'ora di goderci l'atmosfera domani sera".

Che ricordi hai della prima partita vista qui?"Sì, di fatto ho visto qui la prima partita in cui ho preso consapevolezza del calcio. Abbiamo visto la partita della Germania qui nel '90 e da allora la passione è cresciuta dentro di me".

Ti aspettavi di venire qui ad affrontare l'Inter con un punto in più in classifica?"Devo essere molto sincero, era impossibile aspettarselo. siamo davanti e faremo di tutto perché l'Inter rimanga dietro. Ci vogliamo misurare contro loro, sono tra le più grandi squadre in Europa. Noi siamo in grado di tenere testa anche ad avversarie così forti".

Ha visto Atalanta-Inter?"Ho visto Atalanta-Inter live, ma anche altre partite in differita. L'Inter è organizzata benissimo, ha un grandissimo allenatore e grandi individualità. Riesce a trovare una commistione tra la grande organizzazione, con schemi molto chiari, e il valore dei singoli. Vedendola da lontano la vedo come un unico collettivo che funziona molto bene, ha tutte le corde accordate quando i singoli si muovono d'insieme".

Si parla delle amnesie difensive dell'Inter in Italia."Abbiamo visto che per l'Inter è insolito prendere così tanti gol, non so se ci siano falle strutturali. Non penso che l'Inter giocherà con la stessa difesa di Bergamo. Ci sono anche delle defezioni, ma hanno una squadra fantastica e noi dobbiamo mostrare di cosa siamo capaci, sfruttando magari i loro punti deboli. Vogliamo portare la nostra proposta di calcio, si tratterà di cogliere momenti che saranno magari più favorevoli per noi e sfruttarli al meglio".

Il momento dell'Inter può favorirvi in qualche modo?"Abbiamo visto tutti insieme la partita contro l'Atalanta, osservando i loro meccanismi e tenendo in considerazione ogni aspetto. Vediamo che cercano di giocare di sponda sulla punta, magari domani giocherà Lautaro, ma hanno anche grande tecnica da abbinare alla fisicità. Dimarco ha un piede sinistro che non devo neanche raccontarvi, per esempio. Abbiamo una grande sfida davanti a noi: tenere conto di tutte le componenti dell'Inter".

#2142. Mauricio Pochettino warns Man United cannot be stuck in the past as he sets out his vision for the club - and confirms Bruno Fernandes WILL remain captain amid contract stand-off

Published: 04:01 EST, 20 November 2026 | Updated: 13:06 EST, 20 November 2026

Mauricio Pochettino has warned that Manchester United cannot be stuck in the past as he begins the job of dragging the biggest club in English football out of the Premier League relegation zone.

Pochettino faces a huge first game in charge against Liverpool at Anfield on Sunday, and he is determined to make a fresh start at United uninhibited by the history of the club.

‘We arrived with the idea to create something from scratch, from our own ideas and philosophy, in the way that we love to work. I think our job is to adapt and to go ahead a little bit,’ he said at Carrington.

‘The culture of the club and the identity of the club is very clear. It's an historic club with years being one of the best clubs in the world. It's difficult to find another club the size of Manchester United. It needs to be in a different place than we are now, no?

‘But I don't think too much in the past. If we want to create something different and positive, and the people feel proud about that, it's about building our own way here. Thinking on what happened yesterday is not going to help. You cannot be stuck in the past.’

Pochettino’s appointment at United has been a whirlwind after the Argentine oversaw his final two games in charge of the US national team before flying through the night to start work on Wednesday.

Mauricio Pochettino spoke to the media for the first time as Man United boss on Friday

‘I think it's a race to arrive in condition to be competitive against Liverpool, and now our focus is 100 per cent on Liverpool,’ he added.

‘My first target is to sleep, but I cannot sleep – only three or four hours per day – but I think the target is to know the people, the staff, all the people here in the club, and of course the players and try from the beginning to have a positive impact.’

Pochettino admits he has missed being involved in day-to-day coaching at club level, saying: ‘Of course, we missed it a lot. We are animals of the grass. We like to feel the grass, we need to smell the grass and to help the players to improve every day.

‘It's true that it's a different job when you are in the national team, but I think we are much better coaches now.’

The 54-year-old told United that he wants to be involved in the all the key decision-making and transfers despite only being named as the club’s head coach.

Pochettino demanded more control during his five years in charge of Tottenham, and the difference between manager and head coach has also been a bone of contention at United in recent years.

Manchester United travel to rivals Liverpool on Sunday searching for their first league win since August

Ruben Amorim was sacked after taking on the United hierarchy over his job title, while Erik ten Hag refused to give up his power to veto transfers.

Pochettino said: ‘I know that being a head coach, I am the guy that is going to decide the squad for Sunday and I'm going to pick the starting 11.

‘I'm going to make the changes and of course that, in the future, I would like to be involved in all the main decisions that will happen in the squad. That is (why) in the end, Manchester United is a football club, no?

‘During all my (career), when I started at Espanyol, head coach, coach, manager. In the end, it's not changing too much because I think the way that we work is indifferent to the title description.

‘If you are manager it’s not necessarily that you have all the decisions. Maybe you are a coach and have more power in the decision.’

Pochettino revealed that he met most of the players individually at Carrington on Thursday, including captain Bruno Fernandes who cut a frustrated figure towards the end of Michael Carrick’s time in charge.

Pochettino confirmed that out-of-form Bruno Fernandes will remain as United captain

‘Bruno is our captain and I think he's a very important player, but all the players need to feel that they are important from the top to the bottom,’ said Pochettino.

‘Yesterday we spent three hours chatting in an individual way in my office with all the players.’

Concerns have been raised over the players’ fitness and attitude under Carrick this season, with the statistics suggesting that United have not been running enough.

‘That's a good question, no?,’ said Pochettino. ‘It’s a debate that I don't like to go into because I think we cannot talk about things without context, and data is about context. I think it's very dangerous to talk. We are here not to denounce things, we are here to build something.’

Pochettino later shared a message on Instagram thanking supporters for their 'warm welcome' and declared the Red Devils 'cannot afford to waste a moment'.

'An intense first few days. Thank you to everyone for a warm welcome,' the Argentine wrote.

'We cannot afford to waste a moment. All our energy and attention is focused on preparing for Sunday.

'We can say many things, but what matters is the work we do and what we show on the pitch.'

Dopo il rocambolesco pareggio in casa dell’Atalanta, l’Inter si concentra sulla Champions League e sulla sfida contro lo Stoccarda. Per sperare di evitare i playoff servono 6 punti nelle prossime due sfide.

“Se il Como è la sorpresa italiana, l’Inter è la versione in negativo: il nostro club dalla dimensione più europea, due finali negli ultimi quattro anni, lo scudetto sulla maglia e una rosa ampia, in Champions ha un po’ perso la bussola rispetto ai tempi di Inzaghi. L’Inter di coppa di una volta non resterebbe a secco. L’ultima lascia qualche perplessità. Lo Stoccarda non è facile da leggere, pari con l’Atletico e a terra sul campo del Galatasaray. Ma l’altro avversario dell’Inter è in casa: Calha infortunato, quindi Chivu deve scegliere un regista, e Lautaro non ancora in condizione. Inter nella grazia di Pio”, aggiunge il quotidiano.
