#2084. Mauricio Pochettino must fix a club stuck in a constant cycle of failure. The new Man United boss has the personality but the squad is a mish-mash and where he sits as a coach is a great unknown, writes IAN LADYMAN

Published: 16:51 EST, 20 November 2026 | Updated: 17:07 EST, 20 November 2026

In a soft-focus Sky TV interview that followed Mauricio Pochettino’s opening press conference on Friday, the Manchester United chief executive Omar Berrada revealed there had been a ‘buzz’ about the club since the arrival of their new manager.

Whether he was speaking metaphorically or referring to the sound of drones stealing Pochettino’s training secrets from above only he will know.

As is usually the case these days, much about the modern United remains unclear.

But what we do know after welcoming the eighth full-time manager since the days of Sir Alex Ferguson is that once again England’s biggest football club is relying on a personality lift to drag them back to their feet.

This is what happens when you get things wrong for so long, when you buy the wrong players and install executives who appear to be learning on the job. You end up hiring and firing managers, waiting for the one who finally arrives with the magic dust, the capacity to somehow create something greater than the sum of the rather wonky parts.

Listening to Pochettino here – a talented coach and a good person – was to occasionally think back to words spoken in the weeks of their arrivals on this territory by predecessors such as Ruben Amorim and Erik ten Hag.

For they too were asked about such intangibles as culture and mood and philosophy. They too were asked about more prosaic football matters such as match fitness.

It’s a conversational cycle that tells a story of recurring and systematic football failure.

Mauricio Pochettino faced reporters in his first press conference as Manchester United boss on Friday

A drone captured footage of Pochettino's first training session on Thursday

Amorim – back on a grim, foggy day exactly two years ago – was asked about Marcus Rashford and whether he had been right to spend time off in New York days before the new United manager walked through the door.

Here, Pochettino could justifiably have been asked about another forward Bryan Mbeumo spending the wee hours of Thursday morning playing the newly released Grand Theft Auto video game when he probably should have been asleep.

This is the United that Pochettino is now being asked to fix, a club where names and faces change but problems remain the same.

The Berrada interview was actually more enlightening than Pochettino’s carefully managed introduction to the wider media.

Certainly it was interesting to listen to his assertion that nine defensive players in one squad was enough. In football, as United are discovering yet again, it’s not necessarily the quantity that matters but the quality.

One point did resonate rather more deeply, though, and that was the one Berrada – once of Manchester City – made about United’s players needing a manager capable of dealing with the scrutiny of the club and who could shield some of his players from all that comes with it.

The recently-departed Michael Carrick did look rather lost on the touchline in the final tumultuous weeks of his time as manager. He didn’t look an awful lot more comfortable in front of the TV cameras either.

Bryan Mbeumo posted this picture after the midnight release of Grand Theft Auto VI

In the dressing room? Well, he lost the loyalty of his captain Bruno Fernandes towards the end and that is never a good sign.

Pochettino will do rather better at all that. The former Chelsea, PSG and Tottenham manager has a personality and a charisma that could fill an aircraft hangar. His players will arrive at Anfield for his opening game on Sunday feeling better about themselves than they have for some time.

United have hired some confidence and some authority and some experience at a time when they desperately need it, even if Berrada’s claim that they had to call the USA Federation last week to check on his release clause suggested this is an appointment made rather on the hoof.

It is, however, the impact that Pochettino can bring over time on a jumbled squad put together for six different managers over an incredible 12 years that will define him. Here, the 54-year-old undoubtedly faces challenges.

Talking this week to someone associated with Tottenham about Pochettino’s progressive seasons there between 2014 and 2019, I was reminded of the quality that the Argentinian was given to work with.

Pochettino's Spurs side harnessed the attacking power of stars including Harry Kane and Heung-min Son

The French international goalkeeper, Hugo Lloris, the Belgian central defensive partnership of Toby Alderweireld and Jan Vertonghen, England international defenders Kyle Walker, Danny Rose and Kieran Trippier and, further forward, the burgeoning attacking talents of Dele Alli, Christian Eriksen, Heung-min Son and Harry Kane.

It was a prototype Premier League football team, both in talent, age, fitness and physicality. That was why, in 2016, it almost won it.

Pochettino does not have this at Old Trafford. He doesn’t have anything like it. He has some talented players. The fact he inherits a team in the bottom three is down to Carrick’s inadequacies.

But this United squad is a mish-mash and it serves a purpose to ask how many would get in that old Spurs team we talk about.

Fernandes? Maybe. Mbeumo? Probably. That is about it and that, frankly, is where United are right now.

Pochettino has long wanted this job. It’s not a posting of convenience for him. Like others before him – men like Jose Mourinho and Louis van Gaal – he has long since coveted the cachet, the stature and the power of this remarkable football institution.

Mourinho and Van Gaal had better players than Pochettino is being handed and they both still failed, largely because they were shown to be coaches already over the top of that hill that both had worked so desperately hard to climb.

Mbeumo is one of the few United players who might have got into Pochettino's Spurs side

Where does Pochettino sit in terms of his own personal and professional growth? That is such a hard question to answer and will be utterly fundamental to how he travels in the weeks and months to come.

Sitting here fresh from the training ground and strangely seeming to question whether the glass of water in front of him was fit to drink, Pochettino declared himself to be in his coaching prime.

The proof or otherwise of that will only be revealed out on the grass. For all that he can plead valid mitigation, Pochettino was not hugely impressive in Paris or at Stamford Bridge. Before he starts to talk about dysfunctional football clubs, he may wish to take a look at his new surroundings.

Liverpool away feels like something of a free hit. Sporting in Lisbon on Wednesday less so and Brentford at home next Saturday definitely not. And so it begins. A run of 12 games in six and a half weeks that may well see Pochettino crawling into January begging for fresh legs and new players.

The new manager said here that he wishes to be across transfers and little wonder. When he looks at the mistakes made in that regard by a succession of United regimes and executives, he may indeed question what it is they put in the water in the north-west.

United fans will perhaps appreciate that one of the ‘higher ups’ at Old Trafford has finally put his head above the parapet, even if they question some of what Berrada said. That’s been overdue.

In terms of their new manager, they can be forgiven for keeping a lid on any excitement. Many of them have simply been wearied by the cycle.

#2079. Muller: “Inter come il Bayern, tutti danno qualcosa in più. Ma lo Stoccarda può sorprendere”

Muller: “Inter come il Bayern, tutti danno qualcosa in più. Ma lo Stoccarda può sorprendere”

Installa la nostra App gratuita e accedi più velocemente alle notizie

Mentre navighi nell'app, scorri il dito da sinistra a destra dello schermo per tornare alle pagine precedenti

Scegli Fcinter1908.it come tuo sito preferito su Google: clicca qui

Intervistato da La Gazzetta dello Sport, il doppio ex di Inter e Stoccarda Hansi Muller ha parlato della sfida di domani sera a San Siro:

«Non vedo l’ora di tornare a Milano, a San Siro, anche per tirare fuori poi il mio dialetto milanese. L’Italia mi ha migliorato come persona, mi ha arricchito con amicizie profonde, sempre attive».

Lei è stato il primo nei quattro trasferimenti diretti tra Stoccarda e Inter. Poi sono arrivati Klinsmann, Sammer e Kuzmanovic. Come è stato scoperto?

«Dovevo venire già dopo che abbiamo vinto gli Europei nell’80. Avevo 23 anni, pensavo che fosse troppo presto. Sandro Mazzola e il ds Giancarlo Beltrami vennero a Monaco per una partita della Germania contro l’Austria: abbiamo vinto 1-0, avevo segnato e il gol è stato anche eletto il migliore del mese. Ho spiegato la mia idea e loro virarono su Herbert Prohaska».

«No, e due anni dopo, l’austriaco è andato alla Roma: loro si sono ripresentati. Beltrami mi ha portato un modellino di San Siro in miniatura, con una base di marmo, lo stadio bellissimo, con lo stemma dell’Inter. L’ha tirato fuori da una tasca, non era più grande come un cellulare. Mi disse: “In questo stadio tu puoi giocare”. Non ho mai dimenticato quel momento. E quella riproduzione del Meazza l’ho donata al museo del calcio tedesco di Dortmund. Resterà sempre lì, come ricordo».

«Tre aspetti: primo, aveva sempre un grande nome. Juve, Milan e Inter erano sempre molto attraenti. Secondo è chiaro che potevi guadagnare di più, un argomento che aiuta nel cambiare la società o andare all’estero. Però la terza cosa, vista anche oggi, è per me la più importante: vivere in un altro paese, in un posto eccezionale come l’Italia, imparare la lingua, adattarsi a questo ambiente, alla cultura, a un modo di vivere ti fa crescere. Oggi a 69 anni sono convinto che quello che vale di più è la persona, è l’uomo, e non la coppa che hai in vetrina. Chiaro che vuoi vincere dei titoli quando giochi, ma contano di più le persone. Dopo oltre 40 anni ho ancora amici e contatti frequenti con l’Italia. Nei giorni scorsi mi sono sentito con Fulvio Collovati, quando ho visto Bergomi al funerale di Brehme ci siamo abbracciati come due fratelli. Vuol dire che ho lasciato un buon ricordo».

E la nostra lingua non l’ha mai dimenticata

«Già. Ho anche approfittato del fatto che c’erano solo due stranieri, allora dovevi imparare per forza l’italiano. E i primi sei mesi sono stato solo a Milano, mia moglie non poteva licenziarsi dalla Mercedes e mi ha raggiunto solo a Natale. Mi ha sentito parlare al telefono un quarto d’ora con dei vostri colleghi ed era stupita. “Cosa sta succedendo? Ma come parli? Incredibile”».

Veniamo all’Inter di oggi: anche con l’Atalanta si è mostrata una squadra a due facce, come i suoi risultati stagionali. Spiegazioni?

«Negli ultimi anni è sempre in cima, tra scudetti e finali di Champions. Per gli avversari è sempre una partita molto attraente, importante, mettono qualche “percento” in più per fare bella figura contro l’Inter. Tu quindi devi essere sempre al 100%, ma è difficile presentarsi sempre perfetto. Se ti manca solo il 5% gli altri ne approfittano. L’Inter è un po’ come il Bayern. È la numero uno, e tutti gli altri danno di più, quando la incontrano, è una partita fuori dal normale. Se sei sotto tono rischi, si è visto in questa stagione».

Lo Stoccarda invece che squadra è? Ha un punto in più dell’Inter in Champions.

«Noi diciamo wundertüte , il sacchetto delle sorprese, non sai cosa trovi. Non ha una sola faccia. Però l’Inter non deve sottovalutarla. Se i suoi osservatori hanno visto la prima mezzora con l’Atletico, non succederà. Gli spagnoli avranno pensato, “vabbè è solo lo Stoccarda”, e sono stati dominati all’inizio. Poi è finita 1-1, ma per mezzora i nostri avevano un’occasione ogni due minuti. L’Inter non li prenderà sotto gamba se ha analizzato questa partita. Era una mia piccola speranza, però sono sicuro che non succederà più».

Quali sono le caratteristiche dello Stoccarda?

«Sebastian Hoeness è un bravo allenatore e ha il vantaggio di confrontarsi quasi tutti i giorni con il padre Dieter, ex grande centravanti e manager. Se tu hai come consigliere una leggenda che è anche tuo padre, lui vuole il meglio per te, ti dice le cose come stanno. Tutto lo staff è giovane, quindi più vicino all’età della rosa. Sanno come funzionano alcuni meccanismi mentali dei giocatori. Loro fanno dei capolavori. C’è molto affiatamento, e poi un altro aspetto».

«È una squadra con molti mancini. Anche quattro o cinque possono essere titolari. E parlo da mancino, noi siamo meno prevedibili, un po’ come nel tennis. Se ce ne sono tanti, possono causare difficoltà agli avversari. Sto parlando di Chabot, Hendriks, Mittelstädt, Stiller, Spalt. Con loro è un altro gioco».

Vi siete accorti anche in Germania di Pio Esposito?

«Sì, ragazzo eccezionale, visto anche in Nazionale, sta facendo grandi cose».

Dopo lo Stoccarda per l’Inter ci sarà il Borussia Dortmund, attuale capolista della Bundesliga. Sarà un ostacolo più duro?

«Il Borussia Dortmund non è migliore dello Stoccarda. Sono primi in Bundesliga, però con noi hanno vinto con molta fortuna, non è al livello del Bayern».

#2076. Roma e Como guidano le ammende: sanzionate altre sette società

Fiorentina e Milan pagano 5mila euro ciascuna; provvedimenti anche per Atalanta, Genoa, Monza, Udinese e Bologna.

Il Giudice sportivo ha inflitto 10.000 euro di ammenda alla Roma per oggetti lanciati dai suoi sostenitori durante la gara e 8.000 euro al Como per il ritardo di circa quattro minuti nell’inizio del secondo tempo. Secondo quanto riportato da Giudice sportivo, Sanzionate anche Fiorentina, Milan, Atalanta, Genoa, Monza, Udinese e Bologna.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

Alla Roma sono contestati il lancio di tre petardi e di un accendino sul terreno di gioco, oltre a un fumogeno e una bottiglietta di plastica nel recinto di gioco. L’ammenda da 10.000 euro è stata attenuata ai sensi dell’articolo 29, comma 1, lettera b) del CGS.

Per l’Atalanta la sanzione è di 4.000 euro. I suoi sostenitori hanno lanciato un accendino sul terreno di gioco e due bottigliette nel recinto di gioco. Anche in questo caso l’ammenda è stata attenuata in base all’articolo 29, comma 1, lettera b) del CGS.

Il Como è stato multato di 8.000 euro a titolo di responsabilità oggettiva per aver provocato ingiustificatamente un ritardo di circa quattro minuti nell’inizio del secondo tempo. Il provvedimento segnala la recidiva reiterata.

Ammende di 5.000 euro ciascuna per Fiorentina e Milan, sempre a titolo di responsabilità oggettiva. La Fiorentina ha causato ingiustificatamente un ritardo di circa due minuti nell’inizio del secondo tempo; il Milan, nella stessa fase della gara, un ritardo di circa tre minuti. Per entrambe è indicata la recidiva.

Il Genoa deve pagare 3.000 euro per aver provocato ingiustificatamente, a titolo di responsabilità oggettiva, un ritardo di circa due minuti nell’inizio del primo tempo. Stessa cifra per il Monza, al quale è contestato un ritardo ingiustificato di circa due minuti nell’inizio della ripresa, sempre a titolo di responsabilità oggettiva.

#2075. Ferran Torres acquista gli uffici del Valencia: il club resterà in affitto

Ferran Torres investe nel Valencia. Il calciatore del PSG ha acquistato l'edificio che ospita attualmente gli uffici del club spagnolo, situato nelle vicinanze dello stadio Mestalla, per una cifra vicina ai 7 milioni di euro. La notizia, anticipata da Valencia Plaza e confermata da MARCA, riguarda un'operazione formalizzata lunedì che permetterà alla società valenciana di ottenere nuove risorse economiche mentre prosegue il percorso che porterà al trasferimento delle attività aziendali al Nou Mestalla.

La compravendita riguarda l'immobile situato al numero 2 di Plaza del Valencia CF, dove si trovano attualmente gli uffici della società. Si tratta di una struttura di circa 5.000 metri quadrati, distribuiti tra il piano terra e cinque piani. Il Valencia continuerà a utilizzarla in regime di affitto fino a quando non sarà completato il trasferimento nelle nuove strutture di Avenida de las Cortes Valencianas.

L'operazione rientra nel processo di riorganizzazione del patrimonio immobiliare che accompagna la futura apertura del Nou Mestalla. Una volta che il nuovo impianto sarà operativo, il club trasferirà lì i propri uffici e gli altri servizi legati alla società, tra cui il negozio ufficiale e la sede dell'Associazione dei Calciatori del Valencia CF.

La vendita rappresenta quindi una nuova fonte di liquidità per il Valencia, senza modificare nell'immediato la normale attività della società. L'edificio dispone inoltre di circa 1.700 metri quadrati con destinazione d'uso residenziale consentita, una caratteristica che ne aumenta l'attrattiva in vista di eventuali sviluppi futuri.

#2073. Roberto De Zerbi defends Mykhailo Mudryk after on-loan star angered Spurs fans with interview gaffes - including admitting he wanted to join Arsenal

Published: 17:30 EST, 20 November 2026 | Updated: 17:35 EST, 20 November 2026

Mykhailo Mudryk is still two weeks away from a Tottenham return after a suffering setback to his recovery from an ankle injury.

Mudryk, who has played only 16 minutes since joining on loan from Chelsea, clashed with the Ukrainian FA this week when he blamed them for his positive drug test which ruled him out of football for almost two years.

He also risked the wrath of Spurs fans by admitting he only made the move across London to recover his fitness before returning to Stamford Bridge where Blues boss Xabi Alonso was 'counting on him'.

And he managed to annoy both Chelsea and Spurs fans by revealing, during the same YouTube interview, that he wanted to sign for Arsenal when he moved to the Premier League in January 2023 but his agent forced through the move with the best commission.

'I can understand the fans, but Mudryk is a good guy,' said Spurs boss De Zerbi, who worked with the Ukraine international at Shakhtar Dontetsk and convinced him to resist interest from Brentford and Coventry and be reunited in North London.

'He's a good player. He suffered a lot for the situation in the last couple of years. He has just one thing to do, show on the pitch his qualities, show on the pitch that now he's playing for Tottenham. Tottenham is a big club, like the other clubs, not less at all.'

Mykhailo Mudryk admitted he joined Tottenham to regain fitness before returning to Chelsea, while also stating he had wanted to sign for Arsenal in 2023 before his transfer to the Blues

Roberto De Zerbi acknowledged Tottenham fans' views on Mudryk's comments, but insisted the winger has 'suffered a lot' following his doping ban

Chelsea paid £86million for Mudryk but he made little impression before he was hit with a four-year ban in 2024, which was wiped out this summer enabling him to return to competition.

'He has no responsibility in this part,' said De Zerbi. 'Now his focus is just on the ball or just on the football. He has a big chance to play with us because Tottenham is a big club. He knows very well the coach, he knows very well the style of play.

'He knows I give him total confidence to show what he is. So to move the focus in the right direction, in a good direction. But I think he's working hard to recover and to play earlier.'

Mudryk made his Spurs debut as a sub at Nottingham Forest in September but injured an ankle in training a few days later. He was initially expected to be out for two months with Saturday's game against Ipswich his target to return.

'The plan was to be available for Ipswich or Sunderland,' said De Zerbi, who will serve a touchline ban against Ipswich for three yellow cards. 'Then he felt not pain but he was not comfortable last week. And they take two weeks more, and step by step we're going to see the situation.'

James Maddison's limited involvement for Tottenham relates to a niggling hip injury dating back to pre-season.

Maddison has been an unused sub in two Spurs games since limping off when the problem flared up at Chelsea, on his second start of the season and his second start in six days.

Roberto De Zerbi confirmed ahead of Saturday's game against Ipswich that it had been the same problem which dogged Maddison through the summer as he fought back from a serious knee injury.

The Spurs boss revealed how his physical condition had suffered from not training regularly and reminded the 29-year-old England midfielder he could only make an impact in the Premier League in peak condition.

De Zerbi confirmed James Maddison is available to face Ipswich but stressed the midfielder needs to push himself to reach his peak physical condition

'It is not a big injury, but he was not able to work,' explained De Zerbi. 'He had a problem with his hip. The same as preseason. It's not a big problem but if you don't work consistently you lose the physical condition and to play in this level of competition you have to stay in the top physical condition.'

Maddison, who turns 30 on Monday, missed most of last season with a cruciate knee injury and has played only 195 minutes this season. His return was further complicated by a shoulder injury after coming on at Brentford on the opening game of the season.

'He is still young, 29 years is not old,' said De Zerbi. 'He has to push and demand of himself the top in terms of physical condition, because if he is 80 or 90 per cent of his physical condition he makes the difference for us, he changes the games.'

Maddison and Destiny Udogie have both trained fully through the international break and are available to face Ipswich.

'He worked very well from the Wednesday after Leeds,' said De Zerbi. 'From Chelsea to the Leeds game, he worked just three times on the pitch at the training ground.

'If you don't work during the week you risk injury and lose the normal physical condition you need to play Premier League. The same for Udogie. When they work and are fit to work, they have many chances to play, especially Maddison because a special player.'

Maddison's first start of the season came against Coventry when he scored the opener and inspired Spurs to their first win. He barely trained but started again in the win at Chelsea five days later, where he broke down early in the second half and was replaced by Conor Gallagher.

De Zerbi made it clear there were no problems connected to Maddison being pictured out at a pub in Coventry after the defeat at Leeds. It was the last game before the international break and the first of three days off.

'I am angry because next time he has to invite me,' joked the Spurs boss and he added: 'I am happy. I know when my players work well. If they are serious on the pitch, off the pitch they can do what they want. I'm not a policeman.

'James is top professional, top guy, top player. The private life is not our problem if they work well and they are serious, and give their best for the club, the shirt and the fans, and he is perfect when it comes to that.'

#2072. Kim Hellberg says Middlesbrough ‘absolutely’ need to strengthen key position in the January transfer window

Middlesbrough are flying high in the Championship, with Saturday’s victory over Bristol City moving them top of the table. Ahead of Tuesday’s clash with Swansea City though, potential January transfer window reinforcements were on Kim Hellberg’s mind.

And, the Boro boss said that the midfield is an area that needs to be bolstered. His statement comes after a long-term hamstring injury to Rokas Pukstas, while Connor Barron is out of favour and also dealing with an injury problem of his own.

Speaking to The Northern Echo, Hellberg said: “So, if you look at all those things, of course we need to strengthen that in January, absolutely.”

Hellberg is right in saying that a new midfielder is an absolute must for Middlesbrough. The injury to Pukstas and Barron’s absence and lack of favour mean that the Boro boss’ options in the middle of the park have dwindled significantly.

However, whoever joins has a tough battle on their hands to get into the team. American teammates Aidan Morris and Sebastian Berhalter have forged an excellent partnership in the first third of the campaign, and they are going to prove tough to dislodge.

Riley McGree can drop into a deeper role if needs be, but he is at his best in a more attacking role. The same goes for Myles Peart-Harris, so a defence-minded midfielder would bring more balance and depth to the department.

The player signed will have to be up for the fight to break into the team though. They may also have to accept that starting opportunities could be somewhat limited, with Morris and Berhalter among the Championship’s best performers of the season to date.

Middlesbrough spent big in the summer, with much of the funds going towards reinforcements in attack and defence. Will Lankshear and Ashley Phillips joined from Spurs for sizeable fees, and a package deal worth £14m was struck for Radek Vitek.

Berhalter, Peart-Harris and Pukstas joined as new midfielders. But of those, Boro’s new American star Berhalter cost less than £900,000 up front, and Peart-Harris was signed on a free transfer.

Despite the busy summer transfer window, it is clear that a new midfielder is needed. Boro can not afford to rest on their laurels in the Championship promotion push, with a deep and talented squad needed to deal with the rigours of a 46-game league season.

At least one midfielder – preferably a defence-first one – could help push Hellberg and co over the line after last season’s Championship play-off heartbreak.

#2070. 23-year-old explains main reason behind ’emotional’ West Ham summer exit

West Ham sold the midfielder to the Belgian side in the last transfer window. WHUFC gave their academy graduate the green light to head out the exit door on a permanent basis.

Potts, 23, penned a long-term contract with Club Brugge running until June 2030. He has made 16 appearances in all competitions since his move abroad, four of which have come in the Champions League, and he has chipped in with one assist.

He has opened up about his exit from the London Stadium on BBC Radio 5 Live’s Euro Leagues Show: “In pre-season my minutes with West Ham were limited, they weren’t as much as I wanted and then I spoke with my agent and said ‘let’s explore options because this season I’m 22, turning 23’. It’s a massive part of my career and I wanted to play. The opportunity of Club Brugge came along with Champions League football, the best club in Belgium and it was an opportunity I couldn’t really turn down with how my minutes were going down with West Ham.

“I grew up in the area, going to the stadium watching the games. It was always going to be a tough decision but it was something that I had to do for my career. It was an emotional decision because it had to be made quite quickly. It’s been the right decision for me and so far I’m enjoying it, I’m loving life here.”

West Ham are well stocked up with options in Potts’ position. WHUFC have the likes of Arne Engels, Divine Mukasa, Edson Alvarez and Tomas Soucek, among others, at their disposal.

Therefore, Potts wouldn’t have played many minutes for the Hammers this season. His decision to leave for Club Brugge has worked out well for him so far.

The Barking-born man has become a key player for his current club. He is also getting exposure to European football which will do him good.

Potts rose up through West Ham’s ranks and was a regular for them at various different youth levels as a youngster. He was loaned out to Wycombe Wanderers and Portsmouth in the EFL to get some experience under his belt and to boost his development.

He made his first-team debut for the Hammers back in 2021 in a Europa League clash against Dinamo Zagreb. He went on to make a further 32 outings for the London outfit, 25 of which came in the last campaign.

Potts has settled into life at Club Brugge with relative ease. His side take on Liverpool at home in the Champions League on Wednesday night and they are 2nd in their league table behind Royale Union Saint-Gilloise.

West Ham will be disappointed to have seen him depart but they couldn’t offer him consistent minutes anymore due to too much competition for places. The Hammers have seen their form in the Championship slump over recent weeks.

They have recently turned to Scott Parker as their new manager but the ex-Fulham and Burnley man has struggled to make an impact so far. His side drew 1-1 at home against Preston North End last time out and couldn’t find a winner.

#2067. Celtic boss O'Neill seeks Europa League stand-in as Baur set for lengthy spell on the sidelines

By JOHN MCGARRY, SCOTTISH CHIEF SPORTS FEATURE WRITER

Published: 17:30 EST, 20 November 2026 | Updated: 17:48 EST, 20 November 2026

Martin O'Neill will weigh up replacing Mika Baur in the Europa League squad after the midfielder was ruled out for up to 10 weeks with an ankle injury.

The German hobbled off late on in Celtic's win over Kilmarnock almost a fortnight ago with a scan subsequently revealing that he sustained ligament damage.

One of the Parkhead side's star men since signing from Paderborn in the summer, Baur's absence is a major blow for O'Neill ahead of a defining period in the season.

While the manager will look to the likes of Oliver Sorensen and Joel van den Berg to step up domestically, he will also consider using a UEFA rule to make a change to his European squad ahead of Thursday's match away to Torreense in Portugal.

The rule allows clubs to make an alteration to the squad they submitted for the group stage if the injury is expected to sideline the player for more than 60 days from when it occurred.

Van den Berg, Jota, Landon Emenalo and Shim Mheuka weren't on the original 25-man list so would be options to be drafted in.

German midfielder Mika Baur could miss up to 10 weeks of action after injuring his ankle

'I would definitely check with the medical staff and Mika, for a start,' said O'Neill.

'And speaking to him yesterday, he's really upbeat about it. Obviously, he will have that boot on for a few weeks.

'I suppose deep down, if it was, let's say, 10 weeks, that's taking you right through to the end of January. I can't see us seeing him in the next six weeks.'

Baur's form following his £4.5million move has been good enough for him to win his first full cap for Germany after being called up by new manager Jurgen Klopp.

'He was doing great for us,' added O'Neill. 'It is a blow. And it seemed so innocuous at the time.

'I thought that he'd been kicked, but it was the other foot he'd landed on. It's just gone over. It's a real shame. He was playing great for us.

'There's things that he can't do for a while.'

O'Neill revealed that Benjamin Nygren is also an injury doubt for Saturday's match with St Mirren.

'He didn't train today, but we'll see how he is in the morning,' he explained. 'He's got a sore heel. He missed out the second game in Sweden's run.

'He's been feeling it for a couple of weeks, so we'll see how he is tomorrow.'

19-year-old summer signing Joel van den Berg could make his first Celtic outing this weekend

Van den Berg hasn't played for the first team since joining from PSV Eindhoven for £3.4m in the summer with an injury impeding his progress.

But the 19-year-old could be in line for his first-team debut against the Buddies.

'I had a little chat to him,' said O'Neill. 'He trained this morning, he didn't train yesterday. We played a bounce game during the week, Tuesday against St Johnstone.

'He played in that game, came off and he was definitely limping for a while. He had a couple of days' treatment.

'In the overall scheme of things, whether it's tomorrow or in the next week or two, I'd hope to see him make his mark.

'As I said to him yesterday, "When you get in, it's really up to you".'

O'Neill anticipates a stern test from a St Mirren side who often have not picked up the positive results their performances have merited under Craig McLeish.

'The overall job he's done has been terrific, really terrific,' said the Celtic manager.

'They're having a wee tough time at this minute, obviously, with results, but his overall contributions, I'd say it's been excellent.

'There are some really fine coaches in Scotland, genuinely, and I don't want to sound patronising or sycophantic, but there are.

'We were up against one two weeks ago against Kilmarnock [Neil McCann], so I've got a lot of time for those boys who are coming through and who, to my mind, are every bit as good of coaches as quite a number down south.'

#2066. 25-year-old set for starting XI recall as Sheffield United bid to end 17-year Southampton hoodoo

Sheffield United face Southampton in the Championship tonight. The Blades come into the game in good form, having defeated Watford 4-0 on Saturday.

SUFC have lost one of their last six league matches, and at Bramall Lane, they’ll be eager to give the promotion-pushing Saints a good test. However, Chris Wilder will have to undo a 17-year hoodoo to do so, and he’ll be without captain and key defender Japhet Tanganga for the tie.

Tanganga picked up his fifth booking of the season in the Watford victory and will serve his one-match suspension against Southampton. It means that 25-year-old Mark McGuinness is set to be recalled to the Sheffield United starting XI as they look for a first league point against the South Coast side since 2009.

McGuinness was touted for a move away from Bramall Lane in the summer, but he ended up staying put. He’s had a part to play at Sheffield United despite the past uncertainty too, starting seven Championship games so far this term.

However, summer signing Liam Kitching has become the preferred centre-back partner for skipper Tanganga. That partnership has been increasingly impressive, providing a solid defensive foundation for SUFC to build on in their improved form of late.

But, barring a surprise call to start Jamal Baptiste for the first time since September 19th, it will be McGuinness who lines up against Southampton. The Irishman will hope his strong aerial presence and no-nonsense, box-guarding defensive style can help keep the Saints and their dangerous attackers at bay.

The likely partnership of McGuinness and Kitching will have to deal with the fit-again Cyle Larin, who scored on his return to the side against Derby County on Saturday. The Canadian international now has nine goals to his name in 14 games across all competitions this season.

Sheffield United are in for a tough task tonight, there can be no illusions over that.

Southampton have history on their side in this fixture with six consecutive league wins over the Blades since 2009. The last time SUFC were triumphant in this fixture, a late goal from Jamie Ward secured a 2-1 triumph at St Mary’s.

17 years on, the Saints are under Tonda Eckert‘s lead and pushing for Championship promotion. They sit 6th in the table, but would be 3rd if it wasn’t for their four-point deduction as part of the Spygate saga.

The visitors are the favourites, but they are not invincible away from home – far from it, in fact. Southampton have won two, drawn three and lost three of their eight league matches on the road this term, so SUFC certainly have an opening there.

McGuinness will have to step up and fill Tanganga’s shoes to deal with Larin and the rest of the Saints’ attacking threats. If they can do so, Sheffield United might just pull off an impressive win on Tuesday night.

Mister Gian Piero Gasperini, intervenuto in conferenza stampa alla vigilia del big match di Champions col Paris Saint-Germain, è tornato anche sui risultati negativi arrivati contro Real Madrid e Manchester United nonostante le prestazioni positive dei suoi giallorossi. Di seguito le dichiarazioni del tecnico della Roma.

Non c'è il rischio che le sconfitte rocambolesche con Real e Manchester United abbiano lasciato qualche scoria nell'atteggiamento della squadra che dovrà avere domani? "Ma questa è la dimensione della Champions, che è una competizione, la più importante al mondo, quella di valore assolutamente più alto. Noi dobbiamo preoccuparci di fare quel tipo di prestazioni, perché quel tipo di prestazioni ti dà comunque sicurezza, ti fa crescere, te lo porti in campionato e probabilmente ti aiuta a crescere anche in questa competizione".

#2063. Sheffield Wednesday must deal with Stevenage’s ageless danger man to secure a third consecutive win under Dave Challinor

Sheffield Wednesday have rediscovered winning form in League One since the arrival of Dave Challinor. The new SWFC boss oversaw a 3-0 win over Plymouth Argyle before a battling triumph at Oakwell against Barnsley.

Next up for Challinor and the Owls is Stevenage on Tuesday night. Boro have had an inconsistent season, leaving them in 14th as they look to build on last term’s play-off finish. Alex Revell’s side are a tough one, with physical players across the pitch.

It will be a tricky test for Sheffield Wednesday, and the key to picking up a third League One win on the trot will be keeping Boro striker Josh Magennis quiet. At 36, the Northern Irishman has been one of the standout performers at Broadhall Way this season.

Magennis moved to Stevenage in the summer after the expiry of his contract at relegated Exeter City. He maintained a consistent output with the Grecians, recording 21 goals in 89 games for the club as he plays well into his 30s.

And, he’s been a hugely effective player for Revell’s Boro too. Magennis has always been a physical presence at the top of the pitch, and the 6ft 2in striker is perfect for Stevenage. His strength and ability in the air make him a real outlet at the top of the pitch and from crossing or set-piece situations.

Not only do these attributes make him a goalscoring threat, but Magennis’ ability to win knock-downs or hold up the ball for his teammates makes him a creative asset too. If Sheffield Wednesday can’t contain him, the Bangor-born striker will be key in tonight’s game.

Compared with his fellow League One strikers, no one has created more goalscoring opportunities than Magennis’ 19 – six of them big chances. He wins 48% of his aerial duels and 44.4% of his duels overall (stats via FotMob).

Magennis is in some of the most productive form of his career. He is a perfect fit for Stevenage’s style of play, and it is helping him age like a fine wine and contribute in the twilight of his career.

Dealing with Magennis’ presence is not easy – his recent League One opponents will tell you that.

However, Sheffield Wednesday do have the personnel to limit his impact. In Liam Cooper, Challinor has a vastly experienced centre-back who will be wise to Magennis’ tricks and style, and he can be key in preventing the Stevenage man from having his way while guiding his fellow defenders on how to deal with him too.

Of the potential starters, it may be Cooper and Sil Swinkels tasked with challenging Magennis in the air. They have aerial duel win rates of 72.6% and 73.3% respectively this season, while Di’Shon Bernard has a lower but respectable 68% (FotMob).

Magennis is usually a lone striker, but with on-rushing attackers in tandem. By suffocating the Boro striker at the source, they can limit his aerial duel wins and prevent him from bringing the ball down to create chances.

It is a task easier said than done, but SWFC boss Challinor must have a plan to deal with Magennis if he is to maintain his perfect League One record.

#2061. Calciomercato, Zirkzee apre alla Serie A: ottimi rapporti tra gli agenti e il Napoli

Il giornalista ed esperto di mercato Fabrizio Romano ha parlato del futuro di Joshua Zirkzee. L’attaccante del Manchester United potrebbe andar via a gennaio e su di lui c’è il Napoli, alla ricerca di nuovi giocatori per rinforzare il reparto offensivo. Le parole di Fabrizio Romano spiegano la situazione con la concorrenza di Juventus e Como:

“Su Zirkzee in chiave Napoli bisognerà capire il parere del nuovo allenatore Mauricio Pochettino sull’olandese e anche cosa intenderà fare lo United sul giocatore. Di certo il giocatore apre alla partenza e in Italia tornerebbe volentieri, vuole sentirsi importante e protagonista. I club sulle sue tracce sono il Napoli, che cerca un attaccante e gli agenti hanno un ottimo rapporto col Napoli, più il Como che se il giocatore dovesse uscire a condizioni vantaggiose e non a titolo definitivo può diventare un’idea. Infine teniamo d’occhio sempre la Juve, perché vuole prendere un attaccante e il giocatore piace tanto”.

I Am Naples Testata Giornalistica - aut.
